    RN-459 — The iteration ceiling no longer ends silently in four of the six conversational agents

    Found during the investigation of the turn activity strip (RN-460): arquiteto_server.ex, dev_lead_server.ex, ux_designer_server.ex, and staff_server.ex ended SILENTLY when the iteration ceiling overflowed (defp run_turn(state, remaining) when remaining <= 0, do: state, with no event at all) — contradicting the permanent rule that an agent loop doesn't end silently. Only the PO already emitted toolloop.limit_reached (RN-166); the Criativo deliberately diverges with agent.error (reason: "limite_de_iteracoes", a decision documented in the code itself — reusing the ToolLoop's name would lie about who produced the event) and wasn't touched. The four fixed ones now emit toolloop.limit_reached (the same event as the PO, the same iteration/max_iterations payload) before returning — no change in the structure of each one's dispatch_tool.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/arquiteto_server.ex:195, dev_lead_server.ex:244, ux_designer_server.ex:121, staff_server.ex:113
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/{arquiteto,dev_lead, ux_designer,staff}_server_test.exs ("teto de iterações emite toolloop.limit_reached")
    • Origin: found by a complete reading of the six servers, during the implementation of RN-460; scope extended by the product owner's decision

    RAG search leaves a trace, and the trace freezes the weights (RN-479..481)

    RN-492 — The dev agent's terminal command runs INSIDE the real container, when there is one

    Engine.Actions.TerminalExecutor.decisao_de_execucao/1 gains the FIFTH output (:executar_no_container): a project in execution_mode: container with a REGISTERED running row in project_containers (Engine.Containers.ProjectContainerLifecycle.running?/1, a direct read-only read of the table, the same pattern as Engine.Projects.Project). Before this rule, even with a container up (RN-491), EVERY terminal command ran via System.cmd in the engine's process, against the shared folder — the container sat idle.

    The command crosses engine → api → broker (POST internal/projects/:projectId/container-exec, ExecutarComandoNoContainerUseCase, ContainerBrokerPort.exec) and runs via docker exec. cwd, when present, is TRANSLATED from the HOST path (inside PROJECT_WORKSPACES_ROOT) to inside /work — the only directory the container sees — swapping the prefix <project_workspaces_root>/ <workspace_dir_name> for /work and preserving the suffix (a dev agent's individual worktree, .worktrees/<agent_id>, included). The worktree itself doesn't change place nor creation mechanism: Workspace.ensure!/4 keeps writing in the SAME physical directory the broker mounts at /work (verified in code: the broker's raizDoProjeto and the engine's PROJECT_WORKSPACES_ROOT resolve the same folder, via workspace_dir_name, RN-109) — the container is just one more observer of it.

    REGISTERED running never confirms the container is REALLY up now (RN-486). If it died or was removed from outside between the record and this call, broker.exec fails and becomes a NORMAL failed_result — exit_code nil, a message naming the reason — never a crash, never a silent fallback back to System.cmd outside the container (that would reopen the isolation vector this rule exists to close). mounted never falls into this output: the broker refuses to bring up a container for that mode (ModoDeExecucaoNaoSuportadoError).

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/actions/terminal_executor.ex (decisao_de_execucao/1, run_no_container/4, cwd_para_container/2), apps/engine/lib/engine/containers/project_container_lifecycle.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/sessions/engine_api_client.ex (executar_comando_no_container/4), apps/api/src/interfaces/http/internal/internal-projects.controller.ts (POST :projectId/container-exec), apps/api/src/application/use-cases/containers/executar-comando-no-container.use-case.ts, apps/api/src/application/ports/container-broker.port.ts (exec, timeoutMs)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/actions/terminal_executor_test.exs (the five outputs of decisao_de_execucao/1; cwd translation at the root and in a worktree; sucesso: false and a transport failure become failed_result; a container without a running row/in stopped falls to the usual path), apps/api/test/application/use-cases/containers/executar-comando-no-container.use-case.spec.ts (BrokerRecusouError/BrokerIndisponivelError never propagate; another error propagates), apps/api/test/infrastructure/http-clients/container-broker.client.spec.ts
    • ADR: 0130, 0134
    • Origin: the product owner's plan, Part 1 / PR 1.6

    RN-502 — A dev agent only claims a task with a running container, and the terminal no longer falls outside it

    Two halves of the same rule: without an execution environment, the dev agent doesn't start, and without an execution environment, the command doesn't run anywhere.

    The claim guard. Engine.Dev.AgentIo.try_claim/2 — the SINGLE claim point — queries Engine.Containers.ProjectContainerLifecycle.running?/1 BEFORE calling claim_task/1. Without a REGISTERED running row in project_containers (RN-243/ADR 0081), the agent falls into :idle, persists, emits dev.blocked_by_container, and doesn't call the api. The guard comes before the claim on purpose: claiming only to give it back right after would leave the task marked and with no live owner, which is the state block_task/4 exists to never produce.

    :idle, and not a new status. It's the only state from which a wake still rescues — the guards of handle_info/2 all match it ({:wake, :became_claimable} requires :idle, :rearm requires :idle_tripped, gate_resolved requires a matching task_id, RN-047). An invented :blocked_by_container would be a state from which nothing rescues.

    In the ENGINE and not only in the api's activate-execution, because the claim has a path no route covers: REHYDRATION. Engine.Dev.DevRehydrator doesn't cast :work; whoever claims after a restart is DevAgentServer.init/1 → finish_restart_recovery/1 → try_claim/2. A gate only at the HTTP boundary would let every rehydrated agent go back to work without a container.

    The wake. A read doesn't notify anyone: an already stopped agent would stay stopped until an unrelated event passed nearby. So arriving at running PUBLISHES — RegistrarTransicaoDeContainerUseCase writes the row and the event in the SAME transaction, aggregateType: 'container' (the third aggregate that Engine.Outbox.Drain now drains, alongside session and task), aggregateId = the PROJECT. Engine.Workers.DevAgentWakeWorker delivers {:wake, :became_claimable} to ALL agents of the project (DevAgentState.list_by_project/1 — the container belongs to the project, not to a module). It's the SAME message that already existed, not a new one: its semantics are already "there may be work now", and an own message would require a new handle_info/2 clause in both servers with a guard identical to the one that already exists. Only running publishes; provisioning/stopped/failed/removed release nobody.

    The second half: the terminal. Engine.Actions.TerminalExecutor degraded silently — container without a running container fell into :caminho_de_sempre, that is, System.cmd INSIDE the engine's process, the same one that talks to the database, the api, and all the other projects. ADR 0134 (RN-492) had closed isolation only on the happy path. Now it refuses (:recusar_container_ausente), mirroring the :recusar_nao_verificado/:recusar_runner_desconectado that runner mode already had (RN-423), and as a normal failed_result — never a crash. mounted enters the SAME branch: with a running container it goes through to the broker like container; without it, it refuses. The :caminho_de_sempre catch-all shrinks to what it should always have been on its own — nonexistent project or malformed project_id. No execution mode falls into it.

    Declared consequence: a project without a container up stops working, and says why. It's deliberate — it's what the rule exists to do — and that's why it can only come in AFTER the changes that give a container to mounted mode.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/dev/agent_io.ex (try_claim/2), apps/engine/lib/engine/actions/terminal_executor.ex (decisao_de_execucao/1), apps/engine/lib/engine/outbox/drain.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/workers/dev_agent_wake_worker.ex, apps/api/src/application/use-cases/containers/registrar-transicao-de-container.use-case.ts
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/dev/claim_com_container_test.exs — an initial :work without a container falls into :idle, persists, emits, and does not call the api; the Noop goes through the same guard; a REHYDRATED agent stops at :idle without claiming; container.running in the outbox goes through drain → worker → wake → agent and it re-claims; and the usual path stays intact with a running container. apps/engine/test/engine/actions/terminal_executor_test.exs — refusal for container (no row and with stopped) and for mounted; mounted with running goes through to the broker; only a nonexistent project falls into :caminho_de_sempre. apps/api/test/application/use-cases/containers/ciclo-de-vida-do-container.use-case.spec.ts — running publishes container.running on the container aggregate; the other destinations and the row's creation publish nothing
    • ADR: 0143
    • Origin: the plan "Name and location on the same screen, and container before the dev agents" (PR 7) — ten tasks of exp001 locked up at once because nothing ordered the container before the dev agent

    The conversational agents' decision artifact (RN-505)

    RN-505 — decision_record reuses the GENERIC pattern of emit_artifact, not the dedicated one

    A conversational agent that makes a relevant decision in a conversation (why it chose X and not Y, what it considered, what it accepted to lose) records it with emit_artifact and type: decision_record — a payload with context, options (list), choice, and consequences, the four mandatory keys in Engine.Harness.ArtifactSchemas (artifact_schemas.ex:82), tool-emittable since it entered @tool_emittable (artifact_schemas.ex:98). No cross validation on purpose: choice doesn't need to match an item of options — free text is more robust, and the generic schema only validates key presence (the same ruler as note/business_rule).

    Why reuse the GENERIC pattern instead of the dedicated one. artifact.project_image and artifact.c4_diagram (neighboring RNs of this one) are VERSIONED artifacts — each emission replaces the previous one as "current", with its own HTTP read route and a dedicated TypeScript schema in the api. A decision isn't like that: it's an append-only record by nature, not a "current" that gets replaced — the same reasoning that already holds for note/business_rule. Paying the price of the dedicated pattern (TS schema, versioning, new route) for a log that only needs to exist and be searchable in the session's event log would cost structure the decision doesn't ask for.

    Declared distinction from open_adr_pr (only the Arquiteto has it). The two COEXIST, with different purposes: decision_record is for EVERY relevant decision of any of the six conversational agents, with no friction at all — it never commits a file, never opens a PR, never waits for human approval. open_adr_pr is for when the decision is big enough to become a real DOCUMENT in docs/adr/*.md, with a real PR and mandatory human approval. A project can have ten decision_record and zero ADR in one session — the second isn't the "serious" version of the first, it's another tool for another scale of decision.

    Wiring in the five conversational agents that didn't have emit_artifact yet — PO (po_server.ex:115), Arquiteto (arquiteto_server.ex:103), Dev Lead (dev_lead_server.ex:153), UX Designer (ux_designer_server.ex:94), and Staff (staff_server.ex:94); the Criativo already had the tool since Phase 3b, it only gained the new type (it's the shared known/0 that decides, with no change to CriativoServer). In each one, the common run_tool/3 gained the clause "emit_artifact" -> EmitArtifact.run/2 (e.g. po_server.ex:279) — with no special handling: unlike propose_execution_plan/ assess_implementability in the Dev Lead (which suspend the turn waiting for approval, ADR 0086), emit_artifact never returns {:pending, _}, so it enters the common dispatch without needing the reduce_while those two tools require. And the five gained the SAME sentence in system_prompt/1 (e.g. po_server.ex:463) explaining when to use type: decision_record — outside the identity text (Engine.Harness.Agents) because it isn't about WHO the agent is, it's an operational instruction about a tool.

    Out of scope, declared. Execution agents (QA, SecOps, module Dev Agent) run on ToolLoop, with no harness common to the six conversational ones — they're left out of this slice, with their own per-module investigation before extending. Tying it to Front 1 of the same plan (the Infra Lead's dispatch emitting a server-emitted decision_record when it proposes bringing up a container via runner) is also left out: it depends on a tool (propose_container_start_via_runner) another PR is creating in parallel — see the TODO in this change's PR.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/artifact_schemas.ex (schema + @tool_emittable); apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/po_server.ex, arquiteto_server.ex, dev_lead_server.ex, ux_designer_server.ex, staff_server.ex (alias + tool_specs + run_tool/3 + system_prompt/1)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/harness/artifact_schemas_test.exs (describe("decision_record …")); apps/engine/test/engine/agents/po_server_test.exs and arquiteto_server_test.exs (describe("Frente 3 do plano de decision_record — emit_artifact"))
    • Origin: the product owner's plan, Front 3 ("Decision artifact")

    The local agent's join stops being mute (RN-514)

    RN-514 — The runner DECLARES what it can do on join, the server GRANTS the intersection in socket.assigns, and a message without the capability is REFUSED instead of vanishing

    Until now the join of the terminal:<projectId> channel was mute on both sides: the runner sent socket.channel(\terminal:${projectId}`, {}) and the server literally ignored them (def join("terminal:" <> project_id, _params, socket)). The whole identity came from the ticket — the role (:runner/:web) comes from the kind` —, and the server had no way of knowing whether the binary on the other side was from a version that understands a new message.

    The defect this produces is the worst possible one, and it is what this rule closes: the message arrives, the handler does not exist on the other side, and nothing happens — no error, no log, nobody knowing that the function never ran.

    Three capabilities, and only what the runner can do is declared. The server's vocabulary is exec (approved command, ADR 0104), pty (interactive terminal, ADR 0103) and espelho (copying the work to a folder outside the mounted base). The runner declares exec and pty, and espelho is left out on purpose: declaring what is not implemented is exactly the defect the negotiation exists to prevent — the server would grant it, deliver the message, and it would vanish just the same. espelho is born only as a name in the vocabulary, with no behavior at all; whoever implements it is session 6 of PHASE 28.

    Absence of params is the LEGACY, and that is a fact — not benevolence. Every binary prior to this change sends empty params, and every binary prior to this change can do exec and pty: they are the two functions the runner was born with. Granting {exec, pty} to whoever declares nothing is reading correctly the only thing that binary could have said. The opposite would make every installed runner stop connecting, which is a change requiring operator action before deploy — breaking/ and MAJOR, by the repository's convention, and that is not what this delivery is. Absent params, absent capacidades, an empty list and a value that is not a list all fall into the legacy: they are the shapes that "this binary cannot declare anything" takes on the network path.

    Unknown is IGNORED, never a reason for refusal. The granted set is the intersection of what was declared with the vocabulary that this server knows — a runner NEWER than the engine has to be able to connect. Declaring only unknown names, however, is an empty declaration, not legacy: whoever can declare said what it can do.

    What each mode REQUIRES. runner requires exec — it is literally what makes the mode work, and without it the TerminalExecutor would route an approved command to a binary that does not execute it, with the failure showing up as a timeout instead of a refusal. container and mounted require nothing (the runner is not even their execution path). Nothing requires espelho yet — the refusal mechanism is born implemented and tested, and does not fire in practice today. That is the design, not a pending item. A mode that could not be read (nonexistent project, malformed id, failed query) requires nothing: refusing on an unconfirmed precondition would be collapsing "I don't know" with "it doesn't have" (RN-088).

    The granted set lives in socket.assigns, never in a table. A capability is a property of that connection — a table could claim that a runner knows something the process connected now does not. It is the same reasoning that makes the :global entry of Engine.Runners.Registry die together with the pid. Only the :runner role receives a set: the :web socket does not get :capacidades and no capability check applies to it — the tab executes nothing, it asks.

    A join refusal names the one missing. {:error, %{reason: ...}} saying which capability the project requires and that the binary needs to be updated. The refusal is fatal on the runner side, with no retry (JoinRecusadoError, behavior that already existed and did not change) — that is why it is the only chance to explain, and "refused" without the name of what is missing would force guessing. The check happens before Registry.register/2: registering and then refusing would leave the Registry asserting a runner that did not get in.

    And the half that delivers value today: a message without the granted capability is REFUSED with a named reply, never swallowed. exec to a runner without exec answers the caller in the same exec_result format that the runner would send (exitCode 126 — "found, but not executable" —, the cause in output), instead of leaving the RunnerRouter blocked until the receive ... after and the user seeing a timeout in place of the cause. pty_* to a runner without pty is not relayed, and the originating :web receives a named pty_error, through the same filtered broadcast the runner would use to report a PTY error — without it the tab would stay "loading" forever, the defect that the whereis of handle_in("pty_open", ...) had already closed for the "no runner" case. Since the legacy has both capabilities, nothing changes for anyone today.

    This is not a security boundary. A capability is a protocol negotiation; what authorizes is still the single-use ticket (RN-108) and the proposed_action pipeline. What the runner gains by declaring is that messages of that type are delivered to it, never permission for anything.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/runners/capacidades.ex (new — the vocabulary, the legacy, what each mode requires and the two messages); apps/engine/lib/engine_web/channels/terminal_channel.ex:208 (join/3 now receives params), :215 (autorizar_por_papel/3), :251 (entrar_como_runner/3, the grant before the Registry), :305 (exigencias_do_projeto/1, which was modo_de_execucao/1 until RN-516 made the same query also answer the mirror destination), :595 (the exec gate in handle_info({:dispatch_exec, ...})) and :762 (the pty one in handle_info({:relay, "pty_" <> _, ...})); apps/runner/src/channel.ts (CAPACIDADES_DO_RUNNER and the params of socket.channel/2)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/runners/capacidades_test.exs (new — the pure vocabulary: legacy, empty list, unknown ignored, what each mode requires, and that nothing requires espelho); apps/engine/test/engine_web/channels/terminal_channel_test.exs (describe "capacidades declaradas no join (ADR 0147 ponto 1, RN-514)" — grant in assigns, join without params, unknown ignored, :web without a set, the refusal naming exec in a runner project, and the two refused messages with a reply instead of silence); apps/runner/src/channel.spec.ts (the CONTENT of the join params, espelho out of the list, and the refusal becoming JoinRecusadoError with the server's message readable)
    • ADR: 0147, point 1
    • Origin: PHASE 28, session 5

    The agents' artifacts become files, in docs/ (RN-523)

    RN-523 — The docs/ folder is a PROJECTION of the event log: derived, per agent, and never a source of truth

    Every artifact the agents produce lives exclusively in the event log — apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/emit_artifact.ex:3-5 says literally that "there is no artifacts table". That stays right for the SOURCE, and this RN does not touch it. What it closes is another hole: that memory did not reach anywhere a person would open. Whoever opened the project folder in the editor found nothing of the reasoning that produced the code. (The postponed provisioning — creating a project no longer creating a repository, and git being born at the acceptance of the handoff to the Dev Lead — opens an entire interval in which the project does not even have a repository, and makes this folder even more necessary; it comes in its own PR, and this RN does not depend on it.)

    Each artifact.* event of an allowed type becomes a Markdown file in docs/<agente>/ inside the project's workspace. It is a derived projection, in the same strict sense ADR 0101 gave the Neo4j graph: the source is the event, the folder can be deleted entirely and rebuilt — since RN-590 via artefatos:reprojetar —, and nothing in the product reads from it to decide anything at all.

    The mechanism is that of GraphProjector, copied piece by piece — its own poller, public drainOnce(), a flag against overlapping cycles, batch of 50, and markProcessed only after success. The outbox row is a THIRD one, written in the same transaction as the event, under its OWN aggregate_type (artifact_projection): Engine.Outbox.Drain only drains session, task and container, and competing for the 'session' row would mean losing the race against it almost always. One deliberate difference from the graph: the aggregateId is the project, not the session — the folder belongs to the project, and writing it there spares the projector a query per artifact.

    The list is an ALLOWLIST. A new artifact type is born outside the projection and enters when someone decides it is a document, instead of showing up on its own in a folder the user reads. Left out are qa_verdict, secops_verdict, task_blocked and infra_delegation_files: they are OPERATIONAL outcomes, and a per-task gate verdict would fill docs/qa-lead/ with files nobody opens, costing the folder the property that makes it useful — fitting in one glance.

    Versioned overwrites; append-only carries the seq. The four types read by reduction to the highest version (module_map, module_routing, project_image, c4_diagram) always write the same file, and the folder shows the current one — the history stays whole in the event log. The others become their own file with the event's seq in the name, and without it two artifacts of the same type with the same title would generate the same file, with the second erasing the first: the only way for this projection to lose information the source has.

    Two barriers against the filesystem, and the second is not redundant. The name comes from the title the MODEL wrote. slugDeArquivo derives a name from a closed alphabet (no return path lets a separator survive; it returns null when nothing remains), and FsArtifactFileStore checks again with relative() before opening. The two answer different questions — "what name do I derive from this" and "what am I actually going to open" —, and trusting only the first would make the safety of the write depend on no future change of slug ever letting a separator through, which is the containment ADR 0130 refuses on principle.

    The projection never takes down the source. No throw escapes the cycle: each item fails in isolation, is logged and stays processed_at IS NULL for the next cycle. Unlike the graph, there is no error here that stops the whole batch — a write failure tends to belong to the ITEM, and stopping the batch would block all those behind it. An event that vanished from the event log is marked processed, not retried forever.

    In runner mode the folder diverts to the managed root, for the same physical reason as permissions.json (RN-478): what writes is the api, from inside its container, and the runner's channel only carries commands, with no primitive to write content. Declared cost: there docs/ does not sit next to the code.

    The write does NOT go through proposed_action, and it is not an omission: that pipeline is the origin of every external effect of an AGENT ASKING TO ACT, and here the artifact has already been emitted and is already in the event log. Passing the projection through the queue would fill approvals with routine decisions — one per artifact — and erode the ceiling that gives the click its meaning on the actions that are real external effects.

    • Where: apps/api/src/domain/artifacts/artifact-projection-events.ts (the vocabulary and the naming rules, pure); apps/api/src/application/artifact-projection/artifact-projector.ts (the poller); apps/api/src/application/ports/artifact-file-store.port.ts and apps/api/src/infrastructure/filesystem/fs-artifact-file-store.ts (the write, with the second barrier); apps/api/src/infrastructure/filesystem/project-workspaces-root.ts (pastaDeArtefatosDoProjeto, sibling of permissionsFilePath); apps/api/src/application/use-cases/sessions/append-session-event.use-case.ts (the third outbox row)
    • Test: apps/api/test/domain/artifacts/artifact-projection-events.spec.ts (the naming rules, the traversal and the collision by seq) and apps/api/test/application/artifact-projection/artifact-projector.spec.ts (happy path, versioned overwriting, and the degradation: a failure leaves the row unprocessed, the next cycle retries, one failure does not block the batch, a vanished event is not retried forever, reprocessing is idempotent)
    • ADR: 0148
    • Origin: request of the product owner — "criar apenas uma pasta por ora chamada docs, onde os artefatos estarão de cada um dos agentes"

    Device key revocation stops being blind, and reaches the live connection (RN-519..520)

    RN-539 — assess_implementability triggers the DESIGN threat model in PARALLEL, and only once per story

    The appsec (Engine.Gates.SecOpsAgentServer.run_design/2, RN-360) was born implemented, tested and with no production caller at all: the only mention outside tests was a comment in artifact_schemas.ex. RN-360 itself declared the gap, and docs/fluxo.yml (role appsec, field acionamento) already NAMED the missing trigger — assess_implementability of the Dev Lead, gate implementavel, the same ADR 0090. That was it. run_assessment/2 now calls it via Engine.Gates.Dispatcher.run_appsec_design/2 — the sixth callback of the behaviour, a structural COPY of run_qa_estrategia/3 and swappable in tests for the same reason as its five neighbors (the caller is exercised by a light test, without an Ecto sandbox, and should not start a real GenServer just to prove it ASKED).

    In parallel, and the assessment does not wait. The trigger happens on BOTH exits of run_assessment/2 — with a test plan and without — and the outcome of each one stays byte for byte as it was. Making the assessment depend on the threat model would make the implementavel gate depend on TWO asynchronous productions instead of one, and would burn two Dev Lead turns where today one is burned: the :sem_plano branch already returns {:error, ...} asking the model to call again (RN-163), and a second wait stacked on it would multiply the window. The threat model reaches whoever needs it through the path that RN-361 already defined — handoff to arquiteto, dev-lead and infra —, never through this return; the :sem_plano message does not even mention the appsec, because promising the model something it will not receive there is the defect that RN-163 closes.

    Since RN-674 there is no more :sem_plano: the assessment comes out on the first call, the appsec trigger accompanies the proposal, and the read of the history serves only the guard below. The appsec stays in DESIGN — the owner's decision that took the test plan out of pre-dev was about QA.

    And only once per story. The model is INSTRUCTED to call assess_implementability again while the test plan does not exist: without a guard, each re-call would cost another appsec LLM round and three more handoffs about the SAME story. disparar_appsec_se_preciso/3 only triggers when there is no artifact.threat_model of that storyId in the history. The question here is DOES IT EXIST, not WHICH — the opposite of the test plan, where the most recent wins because a story can be reassessed.

    The event log read is one per call: the two questions ("is there already a test plan?", "is there already a threat model?") are answered over the same list. Two list_events/2 in the same invocation would be the traffic amplifier that ADR 0060 refuses, and this path is hot precisely because of the retry. An UNREADABLE history triggers nothing: absence of an answer is not proof of absence of the artifact, and triggering there would reopen the re-call through another door. The invalid args clause also does not trigger — it does not know which story it is.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/dev_lead_tools.ex:457 (run_assessment/2, the single history read) and :519 (disparar_appsec_se_preciso/3, the idempotency guard); apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/dispatcher.ex:29 (the callback) and :86 (Engine.Gates.Dispatcher.Live.run_appsec_design/2); apps/engine/test/support/fake_gate_dispatcher.ex:23
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/dev_lead_tools_test.exs, describe "assess_implementability dispara o appsec (RN-539)" — a story without a threat model triggers, a story with a threat model does NOT trigger (the idempotency), a threat model of another story does not count, the outcome of :sem_plano does not change, the assessment is proposed normally alongside the trigger, invalid args and an unreadable history do not trigger
    • ADR: 0090 (which declared the automatic trigger out of scope)
    • Origin: docs/fluxo.yml, role appsec. Declared and NOT done: artifact.threat_model is emitted in the STORY's session (emit_threat_model/3 reads story["sessionId"]), while the guard reads the history of the DEV LEAD's session. On the common path they are the same session — the Dev Lead assesses stories that the execution session itself created. When not, the guard does not see the threat model and the appsec runs again: the cost of one repeated LLM round, never wrong data (the artifact is append-only and what consumes it is the handoff). Closing this would require the api to accept an artifact search by story ACROSS sessions, which does not exist today

    The flag that turns an agent on has to REACH the process (RN-540)

    RN-540 — A boolean flag read by runtime.exs is mapped in the environment: of the engine service of both composes, with the code's default

    A pause is only reversible if the path to turn it on exists. The pause of the Anamnese and that of the Psicólogo (RN-115, RN-117) were declared reversible in THREE places — the docblock of AnamneseSchedulerWorker.enabled?/0 ("Turning it back on is ANAMNESE_ENABLED=true and restarting the engine"), that of PsychologistWorker.enabled?/0 and the configuration table — and the path did not exist in any environment.

    Measured, not a hypothesis. With the dev compose up:

    $ docker compose … exec -T engine sh -lc \
    'echo "[$START_ANAMNESE] [$ANAMNESE_ENABLED] [$PSYCHOLOGIST_ENABLED]"'
[true] [] []

    The cause is the layer only Compose has: it does not pass the host environment through to the container. A variable reaches the process if, and only if, it is written in the environment: (or in an env_file) of that service. The engine mapped THIRTEEN Anamnese/Psicólogo variables — all of them cost CEILINGS (ANAMNESE_INTERVAL_SECONDS, ANAMNESE_BUDGET_MICROS, PSYCHOLOGIST_TRIAGE_THRESHOLD, …) — and neither of the two that decide whether the agents run. ANAMNESE_ENABLED=true in .env was inert: apps/engine/config/runtime.exs fell back to the default "false" and both stayed off silently, with no error.

    Probable origin of the defect: when the code's default became false (2026-08-10), nobody needed the variable to TURN OFF. Only whoever tries to TURN ON finds out the wire does not exist — and that person, by construction, shows up months later.

    The rule. Every BOOLEAN flag that runtime.exs reads — the form System.get_env("X", "true"|"false") == "true" — has to be mapped in the environment: of the engine service of both composes. The cut is that of the booleans and not that of the file's 58 variables because a flag exists precisely to be flipped, and the flip happens through the environment: a flag without a line in the compose is a switch without a wire. A numeric ceiling with a good default (READ_FILE_MAX_BYTES) and a release variable (SECRET_KEY_BASE, POOL_SIZE) are legitimately left out, and that is why the list is DERIVED from runtime.exs instead of maintained by hand.

    The default differs per file, and the difference is the decision:

    • in docker/docker-compose.yml (dev) the line repeats the CODE's default. Dev is where the docblocks' promise is exercised, and a divergent default there would make the compose decide product underneath runtime.exs.
    • in docker/docker-compose.prod.yml it is enough to be mapped. That file turns the background agents off on purpose (START_ANAMNESE:-false against the code's true), with the reason written alongside — demanding equality would reject a conscious decision.

    This does NOT change any product decision. Both flags remain false by default in both composes: the pause of 2026-08-10 still holds, byte for byte. What came to exist is the switch.

    And ANAMNESE_ENABLED=true alone is not enough. START_ANAMNESE (BOOT key, apps/engine/lib/engine/application.ex:82,106-108) decides whether the kickoff/0 that SCHEDULES the periodic tick is called; ANAMNESE_ENABLED (PRODUCT flag) decides whether a NEW round can happen. They are different questions, and the periodic Anamnese requires both at true. The Psicólogo has only the second — its automatic trigger is session closing, not a tick.

    deploy/k8s/ is left out, and by decision. The layer that breaks does not exist there: a Deployment/ConfigMap intercepts nothing, and the variable is written where every variable is written. brabo-config has FOUR literals (service addresses and the web's log level), with none of the 13 ceilings nor START_ANAMNESE — the engine runs with the code's defaults, coherently. There is no wireless switch to fix there.

    • Where: docker/docker-compose.yml:406-425 (the four boot keys and the four product flags, in each agent's block); docker/docker-compose.prod.yml:308-315; apps/engine/config/runtime.exs:157,158,162,168,185,229,238,264 (the eight flags); .env.example (the two questions, side by side)
    • Test: scripts/ci/flags-do-engine-no-compose.spec.ts — derives the flags from runtime.exs and fails naming the one missing in either of the two composes, and the one diverging from the code's default in the dev one. No warn: it really fails, in the test-packages job
    • Origin: found by direct measurement in the engine container, 2026-09-08. Declared: the exception list FLAGS_FORA_DO_COMPOSE is born EMPTY — it exists so that "out by decision" has somewhere to be said, and does not become indistinguishable from "out by forgetfulness"

    The device key gets a screen, and the screen states the reach of revoking (RN-561)

    RN-566 — The Infra Lead refuses container_start by MODE before proposing: the gap of RN-494 closes in the tool that declared it

    propose_container_start proposed blindly. dispatch_container_start/2 assembled the payload and called EngineApiClient.propose_action(...) directly — without a project read between one thing and the other, and with zero occurrences of execution_mode in the whole tool. In a runner project the proposal could only end in failure: since RN-507/ADR 0145 container_start does not serve that mode, because its payload ELECTS a candidate from the Arquiteto's routing (ADR 0131) and in runner there is no routing to elect against — that mode's type is container_start_via_runner.

    The rule is not new, and a second one was not born. The sibling tool already refused locally (RN-508) and the /containers page already branched by DESTINATION (RN-521, acaoDeSubidaDoModo): container and mounted come up via the BROKER, runner comes up via the local agent (ADR 0144/RN-503). What this rule does is give the missing tool the SAME rule — one project read (recusa_local_de_subida/2, which returns nil or a reason) and one CLAUSE per tool (recusa_por_modo/3). Two parallel rules would diverge on the first new mode of the enum.

    The refusal of container_start is an ALLOWLIST, like the broker's own: container/mounted pass, runner is refused NAMING the sibling tool, and a new mode in the enum is born refused with a message instead of proposed by omission. A nonexistent project also refuses — the same clause for both tools.

    The refusal is an INPUT to the loop (RN-163): tool RESULT text, which the model reads and uses to call the right tool. Never agent.error, never end of turn, never proposed_action — the api is not called. And it is not silence: the emit of tool.call now happens BEFORE the refusal in BOTH tools (in the sibling it was inside the branch that proposes), as in the generic dispatch_tool/2 of the same module — a refusal without a trace in the event log is the call vanishing from the human's timeline.

    The reads are LOCAL, and that is the decision. Project.get/1 and Engine.Runners.Registry.connected?/1 run in the SAME BEAM process as the Infra Lead; an HTTP call to the api here would put a network call inside the agent's loop. The cost declared in CLAUDE.md — "fixing it would require touching the Infra Lead's prompt/instruction" — was not confirmed: no prompt line changed, and the refusal text is what the model reads as a result.

    What this rule does NOT close, and it is half the gap: the blind proposal due to ABSENCE OF IMAGE remains possible in container/mounted. The refusal here is about MODE, and requiring a decided image would invert the order — electing the image is precisely what that proposal DOES (RN-491). /containers checks the THREE things (decided image, mode, confirmed folder) because it has a human clicking; the agent comes to check ONE. GetInfraContextUseCase stays without executionMode — enriching the Infra Lead's context is a separate front. container_stop/container_remove do not come in: they are proposed by the screen, never by an agent (RN-495).

    No ceiling changes: container_start remains a real proposed_action, maintainer, never seeded into auto-approval (until RN-671, which seeds it on accepting the Infra handoff), and container_remove stays at the absolute ceiling of git push/privileged command (RN-418).

    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine/infra/infra_lead_server.ex:599 (the dispatch of container_start querying before proposing), :902 (recusa_local_de_subida/2 — the SINGLE project read), :474 (the container_start clause: an allowlist), :477 (the refusal naming container_start_via_runner), :492 (the sibling's clause, with the second question — runner connected), :409 (the emit of tool.call moved to before the refusal); apps/engine/lib/engine/infra/tools/propose_container_start.ex (moduledoc — the tool stopped proposing blindly)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/infra/infra_lead_server_test.exs:422 (happy path in mounted — the broker serves both), :448 (failure case: runner refuses NAMED, propose_action never called, the tool.call narrated anyway), :538 (nonexistent project), :380 (the happy path of container, which now requires the row in the database)
    • ADR: 0144, 0145
    • Origin: AT-048 (EP-020/HS-032) — the gap declared in CLAUDE.md since RN-494

    The container broker in the installation, with consent (RN-575)

    RN-577 — open_adr_pr and open_infra_pr are refused by the agent, before proposing, when the project has no repository

    In a real installation (AT-088, 2026-09-14), a project without a repository — project_repositories, project_git_connections and repo_bootstraps empty — received three open_adr_pr from the Arquiteto and one open_infra_pr from the Infra Lead. The human approved all four, and all four ended failed with "Projeto sem repositório provisionado" (ExecuteAdrPrUseCase, ExecuteInfraPrUseCase). The person approved four actions that could only fail.

    It is not an edge, it is the common case. Since RN-541 creating a project does not create a repository, and the repository's trigger is accepting the handoff from the Arquiteto to the Dev Lead (RN-522). The Arquiteto works BEFORE that handoff — so in a new project every open_adr_pr failed by construction.

    The rule: both tools ask, BEFORE proposing, whether the project has a repository, and without it they REFUSE with a NAMED reason — what is missing, and when it comes to exist. The proposed_action is not born, and the approval queue does not receive what cannot succeed.

    The mold is that of RN-566, with no new rule and without touching the prompt. The refusal is a tool result (RN-163), an input to the loop — never agent.error, never end of turn —, and the model continues the turn reading the reason.

    The read is LOCAL, and the predicate is the SAME as the execution's. The engine already read project_repositories directly from Postgres (Engine.Projects.ProjectRepository, the consumers of default_branch/1 and remoto_de_trabalho/1), so there was no HTTP in the agent's loop to measure or justify: the new question, recusa_de_pr_sem_repositorio/2, is the SAME one ProvisionedRepositoryRepository.findByProjectId asks in the two use cases — there is a row for the project. If the two diverged, the tool would let through what the execution refuses, or would refuse what it would accept. Both tools use the SAME function, with the same text.

    In the Infra Lead the question comes before the HALT. propose_infra_pr does not propose on the spot: it interrupts the turn so finalize/3 runs the WorkflowsAgent and consolidates into a single PR. Refusing after that (in abrir_pr/3) would have spent a whole LLM loop and recorded two completed delegations for a PR that cannot exist. The refusal happens at the interception, and the turn CONTINUES.

    A durable trace in both. AT-048 found that a local refusal left no trace in the event log; RN-566 solved it by emitting the tool.call before the refusal, and here the same holds — in the Arquiteto dispatch_tool/2 already emitted it; in the Infra Lead the interception of propose_infra_pr emitted nothing and now emits, in the refusal branch, with the title and the PATHS (never the files' content). And the WHY goes along: a tool.result with ok: false and erro with the reason — the form the Criativo already uses —, because only the tool.call would say the call existed, not why it did not become a proposal. On the path that proposes nothing changes: its trace remains the proposed_action itself.

    The execution use cases keep refusing as before — defense in depth: the agent's refusal is a layer BEFORE, never in its place.

    What this rule does NOT decide: WHERE the repository should be born. The refusal text describes the current trigger, and it CHANGED: AT-092 was decided in RN-582 (ADR 0165) — the repository is born on accepting the handoff TO the Arquiteto, before its first turn, with the acceptance to the Dev Lead as a second door. The text changed along with it, as this rule promised: it names the new trigger and, since the Arquiteto only works after that acceptance, says the refusal means a FAILED provisioning (the repository.provision_failed) or a project prior to the rule, with the two ways out. It stopped being the common case; the refusal remains, because those two situations exist. open_infra_pr is also affected in a new project by another path (the Infra Lead is triggered by the Arquiteto's handoff, which can happen before the handoff to the Dev Lead); this rule only makes the proposal not be born, it does not reorder the handoffs.

    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine/projects/project_repository.ex:74 (recusa_de_pr_sem_repositorio/2 — the predicate and the text, single for both tools); apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/propose_adr.ex:54 (the refusal before proposing), :61 (the tool.result with the reason); apps/engine/lib/engine/infra/infra_lead_server.ex:251 (the interception of propose_infra_pr asking before the HALT), :594 (recusa_de_infra_pr/4), :309 (the tool.call with the paths), :314 (the tool.result)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/arquiteto_server_test.exs:107 (failure case: without a repository, open_adr_pr never proposed, reason as a tool result, tool.call and tool.result in the event log), :80 (happy path: with a repository, it proposes); apps/engine/test/engine/infra/infra_lead_server_test.exs:491 (failure case: without a repository, neither propose_action nor the Workflows delegation, the turn continues, durable trace), :89 (happy path: with a repository, the consolidated PR is proposed)
    • Origin: AT-088 — real installation on 2026-09-14

    RN-578 — The click that triggers an agent turn answers on ACCEPTING, and the refusal stops being silent

    In a real installation of v6.1.0 (2026-09-14), answering the Criativo's questions form took 97.3 s, confirming readiness 97.3 s and confirming architecture ready 51.8 s — each click held by the agent's WHOLE turn, because the conversational's handle_call only answered the engine controller's GenServer.call when the turn's Task ended. A turn above the ceiling (120 s in the Criativo, 180 s in the others) became a 500 on a command that had worked. And the refusal of a second message with a turn in progress ({:error, :turno_em_andamento}) was discarded by the controller, which answered 202: a "Continue" typed during the Arquiteto's kickoff was accepted and never read, with no trace at all.

    The rule (ADR 0163, which generalizes ADR 0086):

    1. The acceptance goes out right away. TurnoAssincrono.iniciar/3 answers :ok to the from as soon as the Task comes up — after persisting agent.status: working, never before. It holds for the six conversational agents and for the four user routes that trigger a turn (…/agents/:agent/message and, through it, …/structured-question/:id/answer; …/readiness; …/agents/arquiteto/handoff-infra) and for returning a story (ReturnStoryUseCase). The api's status and body do NOT change (201 { ok: true }): they always meant "accepted"; what changed is when they arrive.
    2. The refusal BEFORE the turn comes up is synchronous and named. The engine answers 409 (turno_em_andamento, aguardando_aprovacao) or 422 (sem_regra_de_negocio) with {error, motivo}, and the api passes on the SAME status with the SAME sentence. turno_em_andamento gets a durable agent.error (origin politica); the other two already had one.
    3. The turn's outcome goes through the channel and the log, never through HTTP. A failure stays a durable agent.error, with the same origin as before.
    4. The screen stops treating "the call resolved" as end of turn. After the acceptance, it follows the end through the channel (agent.done) and, as a safety net, through the log's tail every 4 s: it closes when that agent's most recent persisted agent.status is not working. Without any agent.status of the agent in the window, it does not close — not knowing is not "it ended".
    5. The handoff to the Dev Lead keeps being born AFTER the Infra one. The :offer_dev_handoff that arrives with the Arquiteto's closing turn in progress stays pending and runs when the turn closes — success, failure, crash or cancellation. (Superseded by RN-672: the Arquiteto no longer offers to the Dev Lead, and the pending item ceased to exist.)

    What this rule does NOT close: the chat.message the api writes before asking the engine stays in the log when the engine refuses (now explained by the agent.error, before orphaned and mute); an engine restarted in the middle of the turn does not write idle, and the strip stays until "Stop" or a reload; and a message to infra via the composer fell to the Criativo through the final clause of AgentCommandController.message/2 — pre-existing, measured here and closed later by RN-584, which removed the default recipient.

    Revised for the MESSAGE by RN-673 (ADR 0191): the user's message that arrives with a turn in progress no longer receives turno_em_andamento — it enters the agent's queue and is read at the end of the turn. The refusal of item 2 still holds for the commands that are not speech (review, readiness, handoff offer).

    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/turno_assincrono.ex:120 (the acceptance), :130 and :314 (the durable refusal); apps/engine/lib/engine_web/controllers/agent_command_controller.ex:241 (202), :243/:253/:263 (409/409/422); apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/dev_lead_server.ex:211; apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/arquiteto_server.ex:160 (defer), :202 (drain); apps/api/src/infrastructure/http-clients/api-to-engine-client.ts:591, :612; apps/web/src/lib/session-turno.ts:33 (turnoTerminouNoLog), :349 (acompanharTurnoPeloLog), :316; apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx:491, :517, :578; apps/web/src/lib/session-promocao.ts:141; apps/web/src/lib/recusa-do-agente.ts:17

    RN-579 — With the session channel alive, the Session screen swaps the short poll for invalidation and a long fallback; an empty-body response has an ETag

    The installation measured in AT-093 (v6.1.0, 09/14) showed ONE browser making 7,088 requests in 56 minutes — median of 118/min, peak of 263/min — against the 300/min of RATE_LIMIT_USER. Two visible tabs at the peak exceed the ceiling, and the 429 lands in the account owner's browser. Almost everything was a 3–5s poll from the Session screen over data that rarely changes (actions, handoffs and backlog came back 304 99% of the time), while the screen already kept the session:<id> channel with the engine open. The ceiling does NOT change: what drops is the request.

    The rule:

    1. The engine announces every confirmed write. The facade Engine.Sessions.EngineApiClient emits event.appended (only type and actorId, never the payload) after the api CONFIRMS append_event, append_event_returning, propose_action (proposed_action.created), create_handoff (handoff.offered) and create_epic/story/task (backlog.*_created). A refused write does not announce. Before, only ArtifactEmitter and the Infra Lead announced, by hand — and they announced even when the api refused the append. Since AT-157, the write the API does on its own also announces — the decision of a human in another tab, a session transition, the chat answered by the api's own model. AppendSessionEventUseCase and SendChatMessageUseCase (the only two points that write to session_events without going through the engine) call SessionChannelNotifier.eventAppended, which asks the engine POST /internal/sessions/:id/event-appended; the engine does the SAME event.appended (only type and actorId). The announcement goes out AFTER the commit of the outermost transaction (aposCommit in DrizzleUnitOfWork): an undone transaction does not announce, and announcing from inside it would make the web fetch before the event exists. A write that came from the engine (an /internal/* route, marked by OrigemEngineMiddleware) is NOT announced again by the api — the facade already announced. It is best effort and is not awaited: a lost announcement never fails the write, and the 15s fallback covers it. It is only those two points: another use case that starts writing to session_events without AppendSessionEventUseCase needs to call the notifier too.
    2. "Channel alive" is a CONFIRMED join. connectSessionHeartbeat marks the session alive on the join's ok and unmarks it on error/close of the channel or the socket and on cleanup. The state is per session and per tab, outside React, because the interval belongs to each OBSERVER and three observers (Shell, SessionPage, ContextAside) read the same events key.
    3. With the channel alive, the poll becomes a fallback. Events, actions, handoffs, backlog and the session itself go from 3–5s to 15s; the budget, from 5s to 30s. intervaloDaSessao is Math.max: whoever already polled slower does not speed up. A dropped channel brings back the short interval RIGHT AWAY — the screen never gets worse than it was.
    4. The announcement's TYPE decides what to invalidate, with a window. Every announcement invalidates the events and the budget; proposed_action.*/action.* also the actions, handoff.* the handoffs, backlog.* the backlog. Each target has a minimum window between two fetches (events 3s, actions/handoffs/backlog 2s, budget 10s): the first goes out right away, the following ones within the window become ONE at its end. Without that, a burst of tool.result would become one fetch per event. The rule of finding C remains, and only for the EVENTS: during a streaming turn they are not anticipated; the actions are.
    5. A hidden tab does not poll. refetchIntervalInBackground: false (TanStack v5's default, now written in OPCOES_PADRAO_DAS_QUERIES) — measured by test, not assumed.
    6. An empty body has a validator. A handler returning null went out with an empty body and WITHOUT an ETag (Express's res.send only generates an ETag with a body), so a 304 was impossible: sessions/:id/budget came back 304 in 0% of the 483 measured calls and execution/session in 22% (the time with active execution). The api writes the empty body's ETag when send goes out without a body — decided on what goes over the wire, not on the handler's value, so that a handler with @Res() that writes later never inherits the empty one's validator. A 304 does NOT reduce the rate limit count (the guard counts before the handler); it reduces bandwidth.
    7. It composes with following the turn through the log's tail (RN-578). The ACCEPTED turn keeps being followed by reading the tail every 4s (acompanharTurnoPeloLog) — it is the net against the lost agent.done, and the channel's announcement can be lost along with it, so the tick does NOT become a 15s fallback. What changes: the event.appended of the FOLLOWED agent's agent.status runs the read RIGHT AWAY (avisoPedeVerificacaoDoTurno), and the turn closes as soon as the log says it closed, without waiting for the tick. An announcement from another agent, of another type, or without a followed turn reads nothing extra. And the 3s poll of events stays PAUSED during streaming (findings 2/7): intervaloDaSessao only holds when the pause does not, and invalidation through the channel respects the same rule of finding C.

    Numbers (budget test, one tab on the Session screen, per minute): 123 with the channel down (the behavior from before, intact as a fallback) and 46 with it alive — of which 24 are from the Shell (projects summary and execution session, which do not belong to the session and stay at 5s). Two tabs: 246 → 92. Since RN-632 (AT-278) the PROJECT reads poll at the project pace (15s) and the Executors and Overview tabs go through the SAME criarInvalidadorDoCanal — the measurement with the real screens and two tabs lives there.

    What this rule does NOT close: the announcement of the write the api does on its own (item 1, AT-157) is best effort — if the engine is out or the announcement is lost, the screen gets there through the fallback, within 15s (it was 3s); the announcement goes to the engine node that received the call, and the Phoenix PubSub broadcast delivers it to subscribers on any node of the cluster (not measured in a cluster). The roster's working status still depends on the event.appended of the agent.status, which goes out after the api writes it. projects-summary and execution/session (Shell) stay at 5s. During an accepted turn, RN-578's 4s tail read adds up to 15 req/min, only while the turn lasts. It is not a consistency model change (no ADR): the source remains the GET, the channel remains only a trigger — as it already was since Phase 4a —, and what changes is the maximum latency of writes without an announcement.

    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine/sessions/engine_api_client.ex:581, :607, :630, :1034 (avisar_canal);

    RN-580 — The conversational agent that comes up over a conversation receives its END, with questions and tools, and the beginning that did not fit is summarized with the number written

    The six conversational agents (Criativo, PO, Arquiteto, Dev Lead, UX Designer and Staff) rebuild the history from the event log in init/1 — on restart and also when they come up in a session that already has a conversation (the PO at the Criativo's handoff). Measured on 09/13 (AT-073, against b61bc49cd), the six had the SAME copy of rehydrate/2, with three defects:

    1. They read the beginning. EngineApiClient.list_events/2 asks for limit=200 without latest, and the internal route — which did not even accept latest — returns the FIRST 200. In a conversation of 201 events the agent woke up without the message it was answering. The screen had already solved this with latest: true and a cut warning (RN-180); the engine had not inherited it.
    2. They rebuilt only chat.message and agent.response. The question asked via a form (chat.structured_question) and the tools called (tool.call/tool.result) disappeared.
    3. They cut silently. And the kickoff reads (PO, Arquiteto, Dev Lead, UX Designer) and those of the product_brief rules (Criativo) filtered in memory the same FIRST 200 events of all types: in a long conversation with the Criativo, the brief was born after event 200 and the PO received "(no product brief available)".

    The rule:

    1. A single path. Engine.Agents.Reidratacao replaces the six copies; the six servers call historico/3 in init/1.
    2. The tail, with ADR 0060's ceiling. The read is latest=true&limit=200 — the route's MAX_LIMIT, neither more (the api would cut) nor less (it would leave out what it delivers for free). The ceiling did NOT become unlimited.
    3. What comes in. chat.message and agent.response as always (from any agent of the session — it is what the PO inherits from the Criativo); chat.structured_question as the agent's speech, with labels and options; tool.call/tool.result of the agent ITSELF as a text note with the tool, the arguments (cut at 1,500 characters) and the outcome — never as a role: "tool" message, because the event does not keep the call's id and a tool_result without the matching tool_use is refused by the provider. chat.structured_question_answered is left OUT on purpose: the api writes the same answers in a chat.message right after (AnswerStructuredQuestionUseCase reuses SendAgentMessageUseCase), and rehydrating both would duplicate the answer.
    4. The beginning that did not fit is stated, with the number. The seq is gapless and starts at 1, so the number of omitted events is the seq of the tail's first one minus one — SUBTRACTION, without an extra request. When it is greater than zero, the history opens with ONE system message that writes that number and brings the summary of the agent's most recent context compaction (when it was recorded) and the conversation's opening (the first six messages of the first 40 events, cut at 500 characters). Those are two more reads with a ceiling — three in total, and only one when the conversation fits.
    5. context.compacted records the summary from now on — summary, agent and messagesSummarized, next to the usual counts. A conversation compacted before this stays DECLARED ("compacted N time(s) before the summary started to be recorded; that summary was lost"), never rebuilt; the old events do not change.
    6. An unreadable history does not become an empty conversation. If the read fails, the agent receives a system message saying it could not read the history.
    7. Kickoffs and brief refs read BY TYPE, from the tail. The internal route gained types (up to 20, validated; malformed is 400) — a CONTRACT change, additive: without latest/types the response is the one from before. When the read by type hits the ceiling, the kickoff says there may be older items.

    Measured per agent: all six changed in rehydration (init/1). In the kickoffs, PO, Arquiteto, Dev Lead and UX Designer changed; the Criativo changed in the product_brief refs and in the zero-rule guardrail; the Staff has no kickoff (ADR 0088).

    What this rule does NOT close: the text the tool returned was not in the log — closed by RN-589, from then on (an old session stays without it). InfraLeadServer (which rehydrates via Reidratacao.historico/3 since AT-150, without its own copy) and DevLeadTools.run_assessment/2 (which reads the TAIL, latest with the same 200 ceiling) also go through the common path; no reader of list_events/2 without latest remains in apps/engine/lib. Rehydration can bring MORE than the live context had (the whole tail, even what had already been compacted); the ContextManager compacts again on the next turn.

    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/reidratacao.ex:71 (historico/3), :46 (the ceiling), :104 (eventos_do_tipo/3), :152 (mensagens/2), :167 (structured question), :170 (tool), :271 (omitted by subtraction), :274 (the summary of the beginning), :294 (the compaction), :328 (the opening); apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/context_manager.ex:138 (the summary recorded); apps/engine/lib/engine/sessions/engine_api_client.ex:1066

    • Origin: AT-073, raised on 2026-09-13

    The repository is born on accepting the handoff to the Arquiteto, and execution does not start without it (RN-582)

    RN-584 — The chat message has no default recipient: each conversing agent has its own clause, and the rest is a named refusal

    The last clause of EngineWeb.AgentCommandController.message/2 did not look at the agent: it matched ANY name the clauses above had not caught and delivered the message to the Criativo. A message written to infra was read by another agent, the click answered 202, and the Criativo answered in the thread — nothing broke, and nobody saw. Found as an adjacency of RN-578 (AT-089) and fixed here (AT-098).

    The measurement, before the fix (the three sides, on dev at 72ee8cdcf):

    Destination Does the screen offer it? Does the api accept it? Did the engine have a clause?
    criativo yes (composer: the creative session's fallback and AGENTES_DE_CHAT) yes NO — it was the final clause, without a guard
    po, arquiteto, dev-lead, ux-designer, staff yes (AGENTES_DE_CHAT, the most recent agent.activated) yes yes, one each
    infra not in the composer (out of AGENTES_DE_CHAT since the 9-fix finding and RN-499) yes no — it fell to the Criativo
    any other name in the roster (qa, subagents, secops, psicologo…) only through the structured questions form, which answers the ACTOR who asked (agent={event.actor.id}); today only Criativo and PO ask yes no — it fell to the Criativo
    unknown name, or none no yes (the api does not validate the agent; only the sessionId becomes a checked URL segment, RN-128) no — it fell to the Criativo

    The rule:

    1. Each conversing agent has its OWN clause, the Criativo included ("agent" => "criativo"). The final clause stopped existing as a delivery: it is a REFUSAL.
    2. A name without a clause is a NAMED 422, {error, motivo} in the mold of RN-578: agente_sem_conversa, with a sentence that names the agent and says the message was recorded and no agent read it. The api passes on the 422 as UnprocessableEntityException with the SAME sentence (the postComandoDeTurno of RN-578, without new code), and the screen shows it through the mensagemDaRecusaDoAgente that already existed. Without "agent" in the body it is agente_ausente, and a conversing agent without text is mensagem_sem_texto — three reasons, because "does not converse" said to one who converses would be a lie.
    3. infra has its OWN refusal, with a sentence that says what it does: it works by proposal (the infra PR, starting the container), and what asks for a decision shows up in Approvals. It is not missing code, it is the same reading as RN-499 — the Infra Lead is propositional. And the user_message/2 that InfraLeadServer still exports does not serve for a clause: it runs the WHOLE turn inside handle_call (up to 180 s), without the acceptance of ADR 0163 and without "Stop" (via_for/2 does not know it). Wiring it would give back to the click the wait RN-578 removed.
    4. "Stop" also does not guess. cancel/2 without "agent" stopped the Criativo's turn; it becomes 422 agente_ausente. The api always sends the agent on both routes, so those two refusals only reach a broken caller.
    5. The screen keeps not offering infra in the composer — no UI change: AGENTES_DE_CHAT already excluded it, and what changed is that, if one day someone puts it there, the engine refuses with a name instead of delivering to another, and the CI guard fails it first.

    Since RN-617 (ADR 0175), items 3 and 5 no longer hold: the Infra Lead's turn migrated to TurnoAssincrono, and only then did it gain its own message/2 clause, the "Stop"'s via_for/2 and the place in AGENTES_DE_CHAT. Its agente_sem_conversa refusal left; the underlying rule of this RN — no default recipient, every name without a clause refused with a name — is the same.

    The guard that closes the class lives in scripts/ci/destinos-do-composer.spec.ts, because the destination is born in TypeScript and the clause lives in Elixir — neither of the two suites reads the other language. It DERIVES the destinations from the source (AGENTES_DE_CHAT, the agentParaEnviar = '…' literals of SessionPage.tsx and the roster's AgentKey) and requires: its own conversing clause and the "Stop"'s via_for/2 for each composer destination; no clause without a fixed agent in the head that delivers to an agent; the last clause of message/2 being a refusal; infra out of the composer and with its own refusal; and @agentes_de_conversa equal to the set of clauses that converse. Five mutations measured, all killed (the old catch-all back, infra in AGENTES_DE_CHAT, the Criativo's clause renamed, the Staff's via_for removed, the infra clause renamed).

    What this rule does NOT close, declared:

    • Whether the Infra Lead comes to converse through the composer was a product decision, without an owner — taken in RN-617 (AT-141): yes. Giving it a clause requires, first, migrating its turn to TurnoAssincrono (the acceptance and the turno_em_andamento refusal of RN-578) and registering it in "Stop"; and requires deciding what a conversation does with an agent whose contract is to propose. Its user_message/2 stays exported and without a caller.

    • The chat.message written by the api before the refusal stays in the log — and since RN-587 the engine writes the refusal's agent.error next to it, so the thread explains itself.

    • The api does not validate the agent. Refusing there, before writing, would require a second list of who converses on the TypeScript side — and diverging it from the clauses is the defect this rule closes. What decides is whoever has the clauses (decision kept in RN-587).

    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine_web/controllers/agent_command_controller.ex:42 (@agentes_de_conversa), :177 (the Criativo's clause), :192 (the infra refusal), :283 (mensagem_sem_texto), :249 (agente_sem_conversa), :312 (agente_ausente), :310 (the "Stop" without an agent); apps/api/src/interfaces/http/agents/agents.controller.ts:115 and :174 (the documented 422 response); apps/web/src/lib/session-readiness.ts:37 (AGENTES_DE_CHAT, the source the guard reads)
