    • Origin: AT-098 — adjacency measured in PR #594 (AT-089)

    RN-585 — The end of the turn only becomes visible after the turn closes in the agent

    Since ADR 0163 (RN-578) the turn of the six conversational agents runs in a Task, and the outcome the Task writes — agent.response, or agent.error when the api narrates budget, credential or binding in the final frame — goes out BEFORE the result reaches the agent's GenServer and zeroes turno_assincrono. A test that read the state right after the agent.error failed in ~1 of 4 rounds (AT-099: 8 of 30 measured). The question was whether the SAME window existed for the user: did the screen see the turn end and the next message hear 409 turno_em_andamento?

    It does not exist, and the rule is what makes it not exist:

    1. The two end-of-turn signals only come out of finalizar/1, which runs in the GenServer's process, inside the handle_info that HAS ALREADY zeroed turno_assincrono: agent.done on the channel and a persisted agent.status: idle. They are the two, and only they, that the screen uses to close the turn (onAgentDone and turnoTerminouNoLog, RN-578 item 4).
    2. The message from whoever saw one of those signals is served after that handle_info — the GenServer serves one message at a time —, so it finds the agent free and is accepted.
    3. What the Task writes is not an end signal. agent.response and agent.error show up in the thread with the turn still open in the agent. A screen or client that started closing the turn by them would reopen the window; emitting agent.done or idle from inside the Task too.

    There was no behavior change: the order was already this since RN-578. What this RN adds is the order WRITTEN and locked by a test in the six conversational agents — the test stops the GenServer with the Task finished and proves that neither of the two signals came out, without a timeout — and the fix of the PO's test, which now waits for the persisted idle instead of the agent.error.

    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/turno_assincrono.ex:158 (zeroes the turn in handle_info), :442 (finalizar/1, the sole emitter of the two signals); apps/web/src/lib/session-turno.ts:30 (turnoTerminouNoLog)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/turno_assincrono_test.exs:434 (the six conversational agents: with the GenServer suspended and the Task finished, the agent.response is already in the log and neither agent.done nor idle came out; after the idle, the next message is accepted — the failure case is the turno_em_andamento refusal, which the test denies); apps/engine/test/engine/agents/po_server_test.exs:478 (waits for the idle before reading the state)
    • Origin: AT-099 — intermittent PoServerTest test, measured on 2026-09-19

    RN-586 — The turn the engine restart interrupted closes with a durable outcome, and is never redone

    Since RN-578 agent.status: working is written BEFORE the acceptance, and the end of the turn (agent.status: idle and agent.done) only comes out of finalizar/1, inside the agent's process (RN-585). If the engine falls in the middle of the turn, the process and the Task die together and nobody writes the end: the agent's last agent.status stays working forever (AT-156, reproduced — bringing up the server again over that log wrote nothing, in all six). The two readers of that signal take it for a turn in progress: the screen (turnoTerminouNoLog, RN-578 item 4) keeps the activity strip, and GetSessionPendingWorkUseCase counts pending work WITHOUT a ceiling (the third signal of RN-064), so the session does not close by heartbeat. Only the user's next message unlocked it, and only because it brings up the agent.

    1. The working left without a live turn is closed by a NEW event, never rewritten (an event is immutable): an agent.error with origin infra and reason: turno_interrompido_por_reinicio — the sentence says the engine was restarted, that the turn was not completed and that whoever decides to try again is the user (RN-059: a failure never becomes an empty response) — followed by a persisted agent.status: idle. The channel receives agent.error and agent.done, so the strip of whoever has the screen open goes away without reloading. It holds for those sharing TurnoAssincrono — six originally, seven since RN-617.
    2. The turn is NEVER re-executed. Redoing it would spend tokens on behalf of a message the user may have already forgotten, over a rebuilt history; no call to the model happens in the closing.
    3. Only each agent's LAST agent.status counts, and only working: idle is already closed, and awaiting_approval (suspended Dev Lead, RN-284) is a pending decision, not a dead turn.
    4. "Without a live turn" looks at the cluster, and when in doubt does not close. The agents' registry is local; in a rollout the old pod may be running the turn, and closing it from there would kill a healthy turn. A node that does not answer counts as alive. The drain's session handoff (RN-588, Monitor.expect_handoff/1) belongs to the SessionServer, not to the conversational agents: while the old pod is up its agent stays registered there, and the new pod's sweep sees it; the Adopter does not sweep — what reaches the orphan working after the old pod dies is the agent's init/1.
    5. Two entry points, one function (Engine.Agents.TurnoOrfao): the boot sweep in each rehydrated session (Rehydrator, off the readiness path — the boot does not wait for the api), which closes the turn even if nobody sends a message, and each server's init/1, a safety net for when the boot could not write (api still down). A read or write failure is logged and the agent comes up.

    Declared and not closed: the Infra Lead (infra) runs the turn inside handle_call, without TurnoAssincrono, and is left out — closed in RN-617, which put it in TurnoAssincrono and in TurnoOrfao; the read window is that of the session's 200 most recent agent.status; if the api is down at boot AND nobody wakes the agent, the working stays until its next startup; and the closing does not recover what the turn had already written before the fall.

    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/turno_orfao.ex:71 (varrer/2), :81 (fechar_ao_subir/3), :134 (encerrar/3); apps/engine/lib/engine/sessions/rehydrator.ex:75 (sweep at boot); the init/1 of the six *_server.ex calls TurnoOrfao.fechar_ao_subir/3
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/turno_orfao_test.exs:72 (in all six: the working without a live turn becomes agent.error infra + idle, and the LLM is not called), :108 (awaiting_approval is not an orphan; idle and another agent's working are not closed), :124 (varrer/2 skips the agent with a live process on THIS node or on a real :peer node of the cluster; a read and a write that fail do not take down the startup); apps/engine/test/engine/sessions/rehydration_test.exs:46 (the boot fires the sweep)
    • Origin: AT-156, inherited from AT-089 (RN-578) — reproduced on 2026-09-19

    RN-587 — The message no agent read stays in the thread saying so: the engine writes the refusal's agent.error

    The api writes the chat.message BEFORE asking the engine (the engine rebuilds the conversation reading the log, and the message needs to be durable even with it down). When the engine refused, the message stayed in the user's thread looking delivered and the reason lived only in the toast — the screen asserting what the source denied. The two originating RNs left that declared (RN-578, RN-584); this one closes it (AT-132).

    The measurement, before the fix, per refusal:

    Refusal Who produces it Durable agent.error next to the chat.message?
    409 turno_em_andamento TurnoAssincrono (RN-578) ALREADY YES — emitir_recusa_por_turno_em_andamento/1, with a test
    409 aguardando_aprovacao DevLeadServer.handle_call (RN-578) ALREADY YES — reason: "aguardando_aprovacao_de_plano"
    422 agente_sem_conversa, mensagem_sem_texto, agente_ausente the controller (RN-584), without a *Server NO — the explanation lived only in the toast

    The rule:

    1. The engine is the source of the refusal AND of the record. The three 422 refusals write agent.error (origin politica, mensagem equal to the 422's sentence, reason equal to the motivo) and broadcast it on the session's channel — the same durable/ephemeral pair as the two 409s. The thread already draws agent.error (SessionPage.tsx, lerFalhaDeTurno), so no screen changed.
    2. There is only something to write with a named session. Without projectId/sessionId in the body (a broken caller) the refusal stays only as a response.
    3. The actor does not come from the route. infra and the six that converse come in as agent; any other name (which the public route lets through) is the system engine — a typed name never becomes an actor in the log.
    4. The chat.message is neither erased nor postponed. An event is immutable, and writing it only after the acceptance would reorder the log: the turn runs in a Task and the agent.response could precede the message it answers (and a failure between the acceptance and the append would run a turn over an unwritten message).

    The api still does not validate agent, and now that is a decision and not a gap. The validation AT-098 asked for is the engine's 422 refusal, and it is the SINGLE mechanism: the api passes it on (postComandoDeTurno) and the engine records it. A list on the TypeScript side would be the second copy that diverges on the first new agent, and a list GENERATED from the clauses would require parsing Elixir — with no gain for the user, who already sees the message and the explanation in the thread.

    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine_web/controllers/agent_command_controller.ex:547 (recusar_mensagem/5) and :552 (registrar_recusa_de_mensagem/3)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine_web/controllers/agent_command_controller_test.exs:164 (qa, the roster agent that still has no conversation — until RN-617 the case was infra, which started conversing: the agent.error with the sentence and the reason), :178 (an unknown name does not become an actor), :194, :209, :224 (without a session: nothing written — failure case); mutation measured: with the record removed, 4 of the 5 fail; scripts/ci/destinos-do-composer.spec.ts (the refusal of message/2 is recusar_mensagem/5 and it writes the agent.error; six mutations killed)
    • Origin: AT-132 — declared open in RN-578 (AT-089) and RN-584 (AT-098)

    RN-588 — Handing over a session does not let the old pod delete the peer's row

    In the rollout, the old pod's drain stops the SessionServer and asks a peer to take over the session (Engine.Shutdown); the peer's init rewrites the row of engine.session_states. The :DOWN of the stopped process, however, reaches the old pod's Engine.Sessions.Monitor by message — and the Monitor is a single GenServer, which serializes each session's :DOWN with one DELETE per trip to the database, without waiting for the next handoff. When the :DOWN was processed AFTER the peer's upsert, the DELETE ... WHERE session_id — which does not look at owner nor generation — took the PEER's row: the session was left with an owner and without a row, invisible to the Adopter, the Rehydrator and the peer's drain's local_sessions/0. If the peer was ANOTHER old pod (.33 → .28 → novo), it did not see it when draining, and its SIGTERM killed it: active in the api and without an owner (AT-078, the rollout-test of propriedades.yml, ~3 orphans in 13 rounds).

    The rule: the drain marks the stop with Monitor.expect_handoff/1 (the usual expect_stop/1 PLUS "the row is not mine"), and the Monitor does NOT delete the row of a handed-over session — it records in Logger.info that it kept it. Without adoption, what deletes is the drain itself (terminate_unadopted), as it already was; the Monitor keeps deleting the row of every session that dies from another cause and that of the SessionLifecycleWorker's expect_stop/1 (the api has already closed the session). No migration: the row did not gain an owner column, and the mark lives in the Monitor's state, per session. Every deletion by the Monitor now leaves ONE log line with session, node and rows removed — the drain's log never reaches the pod's log (the preStop discards it), the Monitor's does.

    Inference that remains: the instant the row disappeared was never measured in k3d; the reading comes from the artifact of round 35452845830 (no row + a second old pod) and from the code, and the race was proven in ExUnit, not observed. The next k3d failure, if any, brings the Monitor's line.

    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine/sessions/monitor.ex:45 (expect_handoff/1), :119 (apagar_linha/1); apps/engine/lib/engine/shutdown.ex:130 (release/1, the drain marks the handoff)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/sessions/monitor_repasse_test.exs:52 (Monitor suspended: the late :DOWN does not delete the row rewritten by the peer), :67 (chain old → old → new: the second drain still sees the session), :87 (without adoption the row keeps going out through the drain), :100 (Shutdown.release/1 marks the handoff — swapping the call back to expect_stop/1 fails ONLY this test)
    • Origin: AT-078 — intermittent orphan session of the engine rollout

    RN-589 — The text the tool returned goes into tool.result, cut with the total stated

    RN-580 declared it open: only the Criativo wrote tool.result, and without the text — in the other five the rehydration note said the log does not record the outcome, and the rehydrated agent knew THAT it called create_epic, not the id of the epic it answered.

    The rule:

    1. All six write. Criativo, PO, Arquiteto, Dev Lead, UX Designer and Staff emit tool.result after EVERY call that returns text. The payload comes from ONE place, Engine.Agents.ResultadoDeFerramenta.payload/2: tool, ok and — on success — resultado; on failure, erro (as the Criativo already did).
    2. With a ceiling, and the ceiling states the total. The text is cut at 2,000 characters; when it cuts, resultadoTotal brings the REAL size (ADR 0060). The reason for the ceiling: the log is read by every project member and goes into the backup, and a listing (listar_backlog) can be large.
    3. Additive. A new field in an existing type, without a migration; tool/ok/erro stay as they were. An event is immutable: an earlier session stays without the text, and the rehydration note keeps saying so for the call without tool.result.
    4. Rehydration uses the text. desfecho: ok, devolveu: <texto> (with the warning [cortado; o total real tinha N caracteres] when there is resultadoTotal). It remains a text note, never role: tool — the event does not keep the call's id.
    5. propose_adr stopped writing its own refusal tool.result: the Arquiteto's server writes it for every tool, and the two would duplicate the event.

    What stays open: (a) and (b) closed in RN-593 — (a) the Dev Lead that SUSPENDS ({:pending, _}, propose_execution_plan/assess_implementability) did not write tool.result on suspension nor on action_settled; (b) InfraLeadServer had its own tool.result and did not go through this module; (c) the 2,000 ceiling is a CHOICE, not measured over the holdings — no real result was sampled; (d) pending product decision (human TODO of AT-151): the six's tools return ids, lists of rules/backlog/metrics and error messages — none reads a repository file nor command output —, but if such a tool is born, it would write its content to the log; the cut is the only containment.

    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/resultado_de_ferramenta.ex:24 (payload/2), criativo_server.ex:382, po_server.ex:259, arquiteto_server.ex:336, dev_lead_server.ex:407, ux_designer_server.ex:232, staff_server.ex:198; apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/reidratacao.ex:224 (desfecho/1)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/reidratacao_test.exs:128 (text and cut warning in the note; :145 old session without tool.result), apps/engine/test/engine/agents/po_server_test.exs:252 (the recorded event, cut, with resultadoTotal)

    RN-593 — The suspended Dev Lead and the Infra Lead write tool.result through the common module

    RN-589 declared two open: the Dev Lead that suspends on an approval wrote tool.result at no moment, and InfraLeadServer only wrote the refusal one of propose_infra_pr, with a hand-assembled payload. In both cases the rehydrated agent (RN-580) read "the log does not record the outcome of this call" about a plan the user had already decided, or about every call of validate_infra_file and of container start.

    The rule:

    1. Dev Lead: the tool.result is born on RESUMPTION, never on suspension. When propose_execution_plan/assess_implementability suspends ({:pending, _}), the log stays with only the tool.call: there is no outcome, and writing "pending" there would be the same lie it would be for the model. When the {:action_settled, …} of the SAME action arrives, the server writes the tool.result with the SAME text that goes into the role: "tool" message. ok follows the partition of texto_do_desfecho/1: executed/auto_approved/approved are true; refusal, failure and a status the module does not know are false — ok: true on an outcome nobody classified would be asserting success without knowing. An outcome of ANOTHER action stays ignored and writes nothing.
    2. Infra Lead: every tool dispatched inline writes. validate_infra_file, propose_container_start and container_start_via_runner write tool.result through Engine.Agents.ResultadoDeFerramenta.payload/2 — a proposal accepted by the api is ok: true; a local refusal by mode (RN-566), the api's refusal (RN-591) and an absent runner are ok: false with the reason. The refusal of propose_infra_pr without a repository (RN-577) goes through the same module, with the payload from before (tool, ok: false, erro) and the cut.
    3. An accepted propose_infra_pr stays WITHOUT tool.result, on purpose: it does not emit tool.call (its trace is the proposed_action), and a result without a call would become an orphan note in rehydration.

    What remains open: a suspension crossed by an engine restart keeps losing the Wake subscription (ADR 0086) — there the tool.result is never born, and rehydration says the log does not have the outcome, which is true; the 2,000-character ceiling remains a CHOICE, not measured: no real holdings were sampled in this change (item (c) of RN-589); the output of validate_infra_file (hadolint/actionlint) may quote excerpts of the file the agent ITSELF wrote, and the cut is the only containment — it is item (d) of RN-589 with the first concrete case.

    • Origin: AT-190 — declared open in RN-589 (AT-151)

    RN-601 — The engine and the broker apply the api's rule to both service tokens

    The three processes that compare the service token use the same rule as RN-598, each in its own language. Surrounding whitespace is discarded, and a value made only of whitespace counts as absent. In production, an absent BRABO_SERVICE_TOKEN, the public development literal (dev-service-token-change-me) or a value with fewer than 16 characters after the trim BRING DOWN the boot, with a message naming the variable. The last two refusals also apply to BRABO_SERVICE_TOKEN_PREVIOUS when it is set. A previous equal to the current one does not count as rotation. Outside production only the trim applies, and an empty current one falls back to the dev default.

    Before, the engine read both raw in config/runtime.exs: no trim, no floor, and in production it started with the public default when the variable was missing (and with the empty string when the compose passed it empty). Only the api refusing to start protected the installation. The broker already applied the rule to the current one, but to the previous only the trim.

    Two divergences of form, declared. In the engine, "production" is config_env() == :prod (the release), and not NODE_ENV, which it does not read; the production image is always a :prod release. And the rule stays inline in runtime.exs, not in a lib/ module, because in a release that file runs in a config provider before the application code is loaded.

    Consequence: every process that evaluates that runtime.exs in a :prod release needs the token, including the migration (bin/engine eval Engine.Release.migrate()). That is why the migrate-engine service of the production and installation composes started receiving BRABO_SERVICE_TOKEN and BRABO_SERVICE_TOKEN_PREVIOUS, without default. The CI smoke showed it: without them, the migration died before migrating. The Kubernetes migration Job already received them through the envFrom of brabo-secrets.

    • Where: apps/engine/config/runtime.exs:85 (exigir_token_de_servico_de_producao), :105 (service_token), :121 (service_token_previous); apps/broker/src/config.ts:89 (anterior), :125 (exigirTokenDeProducao)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/runtime_service_token_test.exs (evaluates the real runtime.exs via Config.Reader.read!/2, with env: :prod and :dev); apps/broker/src/config.spec.ts (describe "lerConfiguracao — produção, o token ANTERIOR (RN-601)")
    • Edge case: the rule is copied in the three processes, not shared: they are three languages or runtimes, and no test checks that the three copies remain equal. The floor counts characters (String.length in Elixir, .length in TS), which only differs for a token outside ASCII.
    • Origin: AT-216 (finding from AT-205)

    RN-610 — The Infra Lead refuses the start by STATE, in the order of /containers: already up, no image, folder never confirmed

    RN-566 gave the Infra Lead's two start tools the refusal by MODE and declared the rest: /containers checks three things before offering the button (decided image, mode, confirmed folder) and the agent checked one. The maintainer's decision (AT-142, 2026-09-27) was to close that distance through local refusals at dispatch, in the mold of RN-566/RN-577, and NOT through enriched context — GetInfraContextUseCase does not change.

    recusa_local_de_subida/2 still reads the project ONCE. First the MODE (recusa_por_modo/2, RN-566's rule with no text change) — it is what says which tool to use, and answering "image missing" to whoever called the wrong tool would point to the wrong door. Then the STATE (recusa_por_estado/3), one clause per reason, in the SAME order as decidirSubida (apps/web/src/routes/containers-subida.ts), evaluated only until the first refusal:

    Clause propose_container_start container_start_via_runner Local read
    container already REGISTERED running/provisioning (ja_esta_de_pe) refuses refuses project_containers.status
    no image decided (sem_imagem_decidida) does not refuse refuses an artifact.project_image exists in some session of the project
    folder never confirmed (runner_nunca_confirmou) — refuses null projects.workspace_verified_at
    no runner connected now — refuses (already existed, RN-508) Engine.Runners.Registry

    Why propose_container_start does not refuse by image. Electing the image is what that proposal DOES (RN-491): requiring a decision before would invert the order. The screen checks image in both modes because its button elects nothing. container_start_via_runner does not elect — it starts the ALREADY decided image, and ExecuteContainerStartViaRunnerUseCase fails without it —, so there the clause holds. The predicate is the SAME as the api's (ObterContainerDoProjetoUseCase): the EXISTENCE of the event, never the validity of the payload, because the api degrades an unreadable payload to the default instead of treating it as absence.

    Why "already up" refuses, if the execution would not fail. SubirCicloDeVidaDoContainerUseCase is idempotent over provisioning/ running, so the approved proposal would be a nothing that spent a human decision — and in container/mounted worse than nothing: it would elect a new version of artifact.project_image that the container that is up, with the version frozen on the row (RN-245), would not use. Registered is not observed (RN-486): the text does not assert the container is alive, it says what to do when it died from outside. stopped, failed and removed do not refuse. A query error returns nil and falls into the usual path — "I could not look" never becomes a refusal.

    The texts, which are the tool result the model reads (RN-163) — never agent.error, never end of turn, with tool.call/tool.result (ok: false) in the event log through the same registrar_resultado/4 of RN-593:

    • already up: "o container deste projeto já está REGISTRADO como <status> — subir não é a próxima ação, e <tool> não foi proposta. O registro não é observação (RN-486): se o container morreu por fora, ou se a imagem precisa mudar, diga ao usuário que parar ou remover é pela página /containers; só depois disso uma nova subida faz sentido. Não repita a chamada agora."
    • no image: "nenhuma imagem de container foi decidida para este projeto (artifact.project_image, RN-105) — container_start_via_runner sobe a imagem JÁ decidida e não elege nenhuma, então aprovada ela só poderia falhar, e não foi proposta. Quem decide a imagem é o Arquiteto (choose_project_image): diga isso ao usuário e não repita a chamada até haver decisão."
    • folder never confirmed: "a pasta deste projeto nunca foi confirmada por um agente local (workspace_verified_at vazio, RN-423) — nenhum brabo-runner jamais conectou a ele, e container_start_via_runner não foi proposta. Peça ao usuário para rodar brabo-runner --project <id> --dir <pasta> na máquina dele: a confirmação acontece quando o runner conecta."

    No prompt line changed, and no measurement with a real model was done in this rule: the premise that the model understands the refusal is that of RN-566.

    What NO clause checks, declared: an installation without a broker (the engine does not read BROKER_URL; what refuses is the api when proposing, with 409 sem_broker_na_instalacao, RN-591) and role/session (the agent is not the one who clicks). The folder clause uses the stamp, which is not a heartbeat (RN-468) — that is why the connected runner one stays after it.

    RN-617 — The Infra Lead converses through the composer, and its turn goes through TurnoAssincrono: immediate acceptance, "Stop", orphan turn closed

    The decision RN-584 left open — "whether the Infra Lead comes to converse through the composer" — was taken by the maintainer (AT-141, 2026-09-27): yes, and it becomes the SEVENTH conversational agent (ADR 0175). The condition RN-584 wrote came first: its turn stopped running INSIDE handle_call/handle_cast and went through the SAME Engine.Agents.TurnoAssincrono as the other six; only after that was the message/2 clause born.

    Stage 1 — the turn.

    1. The Infra Lead's three turns come up in a Task — the kickoff (still a cast, only on the FRESH start of the accepted handoff), the gate correction ({:correct, _}) and the composer message. The message answers on ACCEPTANCE, with agent.status: working written BEFORE (RN-578); the outcome goes through the channel and, when it is a failure, through the durable agent.error. The user_message/2 that ran the turn inline LEFT: the name stayed and it is the acceptance.
    2. A second message with a turn in progress is 409 turno_em_andamento, with the agent.error of origin politica in the thread — including during the kickoff, which before held the message in the process's queue for the whole turn.
    3. "Stop" reaches the Infra Lead (via_for("infra") in cancel/2): it kills the Task — and with it the WorkflowsAgent, which runs inside the consolidation —, writes agent.error cancelado_pelo_usuario and returns idle.
    4. The consolidation with Workflows is part of the turn function and returns the state; the end signals (agent.done, agent.status: idle) only come out of TurnoAssincrono, in the server's process (RN-585).
    5. A gate correction that arrives with a turn in progress goes into a QUEUE (correcoes_pendentes) and comes up at the turn's closing, in order of arrival — the TurnoAssincrono without a from DISCARDS the request that finds a turn in progress, and the gate would be asking nobody for a change.
    6. The orphan turn of the restart closes through RN-586: infra entered TurnoOrfao (@agentes), at boot and in init/1.
    7. The history comes from Engine.Agents.Reidratacao (RN-580), as it already did, and the tool.result from ResultadoDeFerramenta (RN-593).
    8. The iteration ceiling is its own, 14 (the same as Arquiteto, Dev Lead, UX Designer and Staff), and exhausting it now emits toolloop.limit_reached (RN-166) — before, the 14th step ended silently.

    Stage 2 — the composer.

    1. message/2 has its OWN clause for infra, and its agente_sem_conversa refusal left — one name, one path. @agentes_de_conversa includes it; a message to an Infra Lead that is not up (engine restarted) brings it back up WITHOUT a kickoff, and it rehydrates in init/1.
    2. The screen offers it by the usual rule: infra entered AGENTES_DE_CHAT, so it is the recipient when it is the most recent agent.activated — with no default recipient (RN-584). Accepting ITS handoff stays in the dedicated card of RN-499; the thread's card excludes it by NAME (offeredHandoff), otherwise the invitation would show up twice and, being the oldest, would go back to hiding the Dev Lead's one (RN-136). The activity strip gained a sentence for its four tools.
    3. Conversing opens no external effect. The tools are the same four; the infra PR and the two container starts keep being born as proposed_action, with the local refusals of RN-566, RN-577 and RN-610 intact.

    The 180 s ceiling stopped mattering for the turn. It was smaller than the 225 s of the container propose_action (RN-605), and a message that led the model to propose container_start could blow the GenServer.call with the proposal up. With immediate acceptance, the GenServer.call only waits for the acceptance; the propose_action runs inside the Task, with nobody waiting synchronously. The 180 000 stayed, the same as the other six's, as the ACCEPTANCE ceiling.

    Does not change, declared: SOLO_CONVERSATIONAL_AGENTS (generated by pnpm --filter api gerar:areas) is the list of agents WITHOUT an area that manual handoff addresses; the Infra Lead is an area lead and stays out of it, and AGENTES_DE_CHAT is not generated. The Infra Lead did not gain structured questions nor backlog reading — conversing with it is conversing with what the kickoff gave it and with the four tools.

    RN-621 — The compaction summary keeps each turn's original language, and does not translate quotes nor code

    When an agent's context passes the threshold, the ContextManager swaps the old turns for ONE summary, written by another LLM call (the actor context-manager) and reinserted as a system message. That call's prompt is pt-BR and said nothing about language: in a conversation in English, or with two languages, the summary came out translated, and the next model read its own memory in the summary prompt's language — the language drift the language epic pursues, born outside the user's turn. The turn's language guidance does not protect this: it is never in messages, so it is never summarized, but it is also never seen by the summarizer.

    The rule is one sentence in the summary prompt, on BOTH tracks (the inline text and the versioned template prompts/context-manager-summarize.md, which goes up to version "2"):

    Mantenha cada turno no idioma original; não traduza citações nem código.

    1. The language is PER TURN, never a session language: in a session with two authors of different languages, both survive the summary.
    2. Quotes and code are literal text and are not translated — an identifier or an error message translated in the summary stops being findable.
    3. The sentence comes BEFORE the turns, so that the text of a turn at the end of the prompt is not read as its continuation.
    4. Cost: the addition is 76 bytes — 19 tokens by the engine's heuristic (bytes/4), at most 38 by a pessimistic rule of 1 token every 2 bytes —, within the ceiling of 50 input tokens per call the maintainer decided for the language guidance (AT-169, answer 8). The test fails a sentence that exceeds it by both rules.
    5. It is not agent_instructions: it is code text and versioned template text, outside the instruction_patch ceiling (RN-007), because the summarizer is not a project agent and has no per-project instruction.

    Decided without a baseline, declared: the maintainer decided to change the prompt right away, without waiting for the paid validation to show that the pt-BR summary drifts (AT-169, answer 4). The consequence is that AT-167's validation measures the WHOLE (guidance + new summary), without measuring the old summary in isolation.

    What this rule does NOT close, declared:

    • With graph_templates_enabled? on, the ACTIVE version in the graph holds. A installation that seeded version "1" keeps sending the old prompt until it runs scripts/dev/seed-prompts.ts again — the body's hash changed, so the seeder creates the new version. With the flag off (the default) the inline one holds, which already has the sentence. Seeding again also removes from the body the documentation section that made the summarizer receive the turns twice (AT-244, see RN-413); the template is at version "3".

    • The fallback for when the summarizer fails ("(N turnos anteriores omitidos)") is not a summary and does not get the sentence: there is no turn text in it.

    • The sentence is pt-BR. It asks for each turn's language, not the prompt's; whether a model obeys it in a conversation in another language is what AT-167's validation measures.

    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/context_manager.ex:214 (@instrucao_de_idioma), :276 (prompt_inline), :233 (prompt, the choice between template and inline); prompts/context-manager-summarize.md

    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/harness/context_manager_test.exs:417 (happy path: the prompt sent to the summarizer has the sentence, before the turns), :433 (the 50-token ceiling, by both rules), :443 (the versioned template carries the SAME sentence), :453 (summarizer failure: the fallback does not pretend to be a summary nor get the sentence)

    • Origin: AT-166, specified in AT-081 and unlocked by AT-169 (the maintainer's decisions of 2026-09-28)

    The language of the agents' answers (RN-618..620, RN-622..624, ADR 0177)

    RN-622 — The language reaches the model as the LAST message of every call in the turn, ephemeral: the AUTHOR's when there is one, the PROJECT's when there is none

    RN-618..620 decided IN WHICH language the agent replies and showed it on screen; none of that reached the model. This rule is the transport (AT-164, on the specification from AT-081 and the mechanism verified per provider in AT-161).

    1. One role: "system" message at the END of the list, on every LLM call of the turn — tool iterations included —, and NEVER in the history: it does not enter state.messages, so it does not pile up turn after turn, is not summarized by compaction (RN-621) and vanishes with the turn. In OpenAI-compatible it goes at the end of the conversation; in Anthropic it is hoisted to the end of the top system (AT-161). It is not agent_instructions: it is code text, outside the instruction_patch ceiling (RN-007).
    2. One place only. The one that appends it is the EngineApiClient facade (llm_turn/5 and llm_turn_stream/6), through which EVERY LLM call of the engine passes — the seven conversational agents, the ToolLoop (dev agents and gates) and the Infra Lead. No server assembles the guidance.
    3. Turn WITH a human author (the message someone typed to an agent): the api resolves THAT person's language in that session (ResolverIdiomaDaRespostaUseCase, RN-618 — session override

      account > confirmed detected > interface) and sends it in the agent/message command as idiomaDaResposta, an OPTIONAL field. The agent's server puts it in the TURN state during handle_call, and the turn's Task inherits it from the process dictionary (TurnoAssincrono); the GenServer does not keep it for the next turn. Two authors in the same session each get their own.

    4. Turn WITHOUT a human author (kickoff, dev agents, gates, commit, PR body, the Dev Lead's resumption after approval): the PROJECT language (RN-619), read from projects.language on every call (maintainer decision, AT-169 answer 1).
    5. The compaction summarizer (context-manager) stays out: "reply in X" would translate the summary, and preserving each turn's language in it is RN-621.
    6. Failure never brings the turn down. A resolution that fails in the api becomes a log and the message goes on WITHOUT idiomaDaResposta; a turn with an author but no language goes on WITHOUT guidance — never the project's in place of the person's. A failing read of the project language in the engine becomes a warning and the call goes on without guidance. A value that does not have the shape of a BCP-47 code does not become guidance either: nothing that is not a code is interpolated into the prompt.
    7. The texts are code constants, one per language: pt-BR ("Responda em português brasileiro (pt-BR), salvo pedido explícito do usuário por outro idioma nesta mensagem."), en, and a generic one in English that names the BCP-47 code for any other language of the open list (AT-168 answer 2). The text does not depend on the model: switching models in the middle of the session changes nothing, and there is no per-model cascade nor a special case for DeepSeek.
    8. Cost — decided ceiling of 50 INPUT tokens per call (AT-169 answer 8). Measured with gpt-tokenizer, content only, cl100k / o200k: pt-BR 29 / 23, en 18 / 18, generic es-MX 26 / 26 and the WORST case (the generic one with the longest code the accepted shape allows) 42 / 42; the message frame adds ~4, so the worst case lands at ~46. The test locks the text at 160 characters as a watchdog of the ceiling.
    9. Measurement: the chat.message recorded by the api carries idiomaAlvo and origem when resolution succeeded — the event is immutable and says, forever, what was asked of the model in that turn (AT-082).

    What this rule does NOT close, declared:

    • Human chat without an agent (SendChatMessageUseCase) stays out, by maintainer decision (AT-169 answer 3).

    • Only the MESSAGE to an agent carries an author (and the answer to the structured question form, which reuses it). The other commands a person triggers — confirming readiness to the Criativo, accepting a handoff — start a turn without an author and fall back to the PROJECT language: the product brief from confirmReadiness comes out in the project language.

    • Artifact of a turn with an author: this rule declared it following the language of whoever wrote; since RN-623 the same message gains the artifact clause, in the project language.

    • Not measured: the DeepSeek and Anthropic tokenizers; whether the guidance is enough against the pt-BR system prompt is what the paid validation of AT-167 measures. In Anthropic, guidance hoisted to the top changes the cache prefix on every change of author or language — cache is not observable today.

    RN-623 — In a turn WITH an author, the reply is in the person's language and the shared ARTIFACT is in the PROJECT language

    RN-619 promised the project language for the shared artifact, and RN-622 put the AUTHOR's language in every turn with an author — so what an agent recorded DURING the conversation with a person came out in that person's language. Maintainer decision (AT-168 answer 8, AT-245): the artifact follows the PROJECT; the chat reply stays in the author's language.

    1. The mechanism is the same message from RN-622, with a second clause. In a turn with an author, when the call carries at least one tool that records a shared artifact and the project language (projects.language) DIFFERS from the author's, the guidance becomes "Responda em português brasileiro (pt-BR), salvo pedido… Artefatos do projeto: em en." — in the language of the base text: the pt-BR clause is in Portuguese, the others' in English ("Write project artifacts in X."). It is still ONE ephemeral system message at the end, assembled by the facade; what decides is the set of tools that the SAME call carries to the model, which the facade already has.
    2. Why the guidance, and not the tool description. The description travels on EVERY call, in every tool, whether the languages are equal or not; the clause exists only when they differ, and lives in the only place that already decides the language. Nothing is added when the languages are equal, when the call has no artifact tool, and in a turn WITHOUT an author (which already gets the project language).
    3. What a shared artifact is — what the tool PERSISTS is read by other people and by the next agents:
      • emit_artifact (business_rule, note, decision_record) — Criativo, PO, Arquiteto, Dev Lead, UX Designer, Staff;
      • create_epic, create_story, create_task — PO;
      • create_module_map, assign_story_modules, choose_project_image, create_c4_diagram, route_modules_to_infra, propose_adr (the ADR of open_adr_pr), emit_insight — Arquiteto;
      • propose_execution_plan, assess_implementability — Dev Lead;
      • propose_prototype — UX Designer; propose_rfc — Staff;
      • propose_infra_pr — Infra Lead. The product brief does NOT pass through here: it is emitted by the SERVER in confirmReadiness, which is a turn without an author and already comes out in the project language (like the handoff and the revise, which this rule does not change).
    4. What is NOT: ask_structured_questions (it is a question to the PERSON of the turn), offer_handoff (carries no text), the read ones (listar_*, rag_*, read_file, search_workspace), validate_infra_file (only validates) and the proposals to start a container (they ask for a decision, they do not record an artifact). Dev agents and gates have no turn with an author.
    5. Failure never brings the turn down. A failing read of the project language becomes a warning and ONLY the author's guidance goes on — never without guidance, never the project's in place of the person's. An author with no resolved language goes on without guidance, as in RN-622.
    6. Cost — the 50 input-token ceiling from AT-169 applies to the whole message. Measured with gpt-tokenizer, content only, cl100k / o200k, over all pairs of pt-BR, en, es, es-MX and zh-Hant-TW: worst case pt-BR → zh-Hant-TW 42 / 36; en → pt-BR 26 / 26; generic es-MX → zh-Hant-TW 36 / 36. The clause only enters with BOTH codes in the short canonical form (idioma[-Escrita][-Região], e.g. pt-BR, zh-Hant-TW, es-419), because it is the FORM that holds the ceiling: its worst (pt-BR → a 12-character code) measures 44 / 37, ~48 with the message frame. A code outside that form gets only the author's guidance, and the log says so. The test locks every pair at 165 characters as a watchdog.

    What this rule does NOT close, declared: the list is by tool NAME — a new tool that records an artifact enters it in the same PR (the test ties each name to a spec that exists, but does not discover a new tool). Whether the model FOLLOWS both clauses in the same message is what the paid validation of AT-167 measures; the DeepSeek and Anthropic tokenizers remain unmeasured.

    • Origin: AT-245, on the decision of AT-168 (answer 8) and the maintainer's of 2026-09-29

    RN-625 — The loop asks the Jev which tool, and the user's model sees only the menu that remains; the Jev restricts and never decides

    Maintainer decision (AT-236, 2026-09-28) and, on the criterion, of 2026-09-29: before each chat call of an agent, the api asks the Jev (typesafe/jev-1.13, OpenRouter's Decisions API) which tool fits the step, and the chat provider receives only the menu that the P3 policy derives from that answer. The mechanism is in ADR 0179; the adoption decision, with the numbers, too.

    1. When. Only with the turn model's provider equal to openrouter, with two or more tools in the request, with an agent in the turn (except the Anamnese and the compaction summarizer), without catalogoCompleto requested by the engine and with the workspace switched on (workspaces.tool_router_enabled, on by default, PUT workspaces/:workspaceId/tool-router, owner). Any condition fails: no call, no event. With a provider ≠ openrouter the workspace is not even read. The key is the workspace holder's, the same as the chat's (RN-058).
    2. The policy is P3 and it only restricts. Menu = {Jev's choice, previous tool of the SAME execution}. The Jev's responder_sem_ferramenta, or no previous tool in the step's catalog, leaves the WHOLE catalog. There is no tool_choice (the model can still reply in text) and there is no confidence threshold in v1: the confidence is recorded and it is up to AT-239 to propose one. Adoption criterion: 80%, owner decision of 2026-09-29 — Jev top-1 73% (66–79), P3 menu coverage 91% (85.1–94), top-2 90%; coverage is the CEILING of end-to-end accuracy (62%–91% in replay), and the real validation is AT-239.
    3. The state is what the product has before the step, cut from the messages the engine already sends, without a database read: agente = the agentId; contexto = the beginning (1,500 characters) of the system message that the ToolLoop puts in front (apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tool_loop.ex:98, system_msg, assembled by system_prompt/1 at :316); pedido = the last user message — in execution agents the initial message of the loop (apps/engine/lib/engine/dev/dev_agent_server.ex:484, initial_message/2, or the correction one at :523), in conversational ones the person's utterance; and passos_recentes = the last 6 tool calls after that message, one entry per call, argument and result cut at 500 characters — the result comes from the tool message the loop appends (tool_loop.ex:293, concluir_despacho/5, with toolCallId). The current execution is "after the last user" because ctx.messages restarts at each ToolLoop.run. Ceiling of 8 thousand tokens; exceeded, fallback estado_grande.
    4. The turn never fails because of the Jev. HTTP error, timeout (2,000 ms, TOOL_ROUTER_TIMEOUT_MS), network error, unexpected JSON, choice outside the options, large state and collision with the reserved name: the provider receives the whole catalog and the event carries motivoDaQueda and origemDaQueda (infra, modelo or codigo — RN-059), never silent.
    5. The policy does not change. The Jev does not approve, does not deny and does not choose a model. A chosen tool that requires approval still requires it; a call to a tool outside the menu is dispatched as always and the event only records foraDoCardapio. The iteration ceiling is the same as before (the call to the Jev is not an iteration).
    6. Per-step record. The engine writes tool_router.decided, in the EngineApiClient facade for all agents, with menu before and after, choice, confidence, second option, aplicado, fallback, latency, real cost and foraDoCardapio. The api writes ONE token_usage row for the Jev: actor = the agent itself (the spend goes into the area budget, ADR 0110), modelName = typesafe/jev-1.13, modelId null, estimated = false, costMicros = the usage.cost of the response, IMPLIED price per million (cost ÷ tokens) with price_implicit = true — keeps tokens × price = cost of RN-044 without inventing a catalog price. Without usage.cost in the response there is no row. The budget gate is not reconsulted between the Jev and the chat.
    7. Getting out of the dead end. Restricted menu and the model replies without calling a tool (not even in recoverable text), with no provider error: the facade repeats the step ONCE with catalogoCompleto: true, which skips the Jev. In the stream, only if nothing was written to the person. It asks for more tools, never one the agent did not have.

    What this rule does NOT close, declared: the screen (showing that the step was routed, with the confidence, and the "choosing tool" strip through the tool_routing_started frame) belongs to apps/web; the gain is AT-239's; a timeout that OpenRouter billed without returning usage.cost does not enter token_usage; the endpoint is ALPHA and the manual smoke (jev-tool-router.smoke.spec.ts, ceiling US$ 0.05) is what flags the change.

    • Code: apps/api/src/domain/llm/tool-router.ts:27 (MODELO_DO_JEV), :109 (montarPedidoAoJev), :166 (lerRespostaDoJev), :216 (recortarEstado), :268 (menuP3); apps/api/src/application/use-cases/llm/decidir-ferramenta-do-passo.use-case.ts:118 (preparar), :143 (executar), :305 (registrarGasto); apps/api/src/application/use-cases/llm/run-llm-turn.use-case.ts:152 (decisaoDoJev); apps/api/src/application/use-cases/llm/stream-llm-turn.use-case.ts:167 (preparar); apps/api/src/infrastructure/llm/jev-tool-router.ts:32 (decidir); apps/api/src/db/schema/iam.ts:169 (toolRouterEnabled); apps/api/src/db/schema/llm.ts:356 (priceImplicit); apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/roteamento_de_ferramenta.ex:60 (registrar), :112 (custo_micros), :123 (repetir_com_catalogo_inteiro?), :146 (mesclar_repeticao); apps/engine/lib/engine/sessions/engine_api_client.ex:700 (llm_turn), :773 (llm_turn_stream);

    Deciding in the chat, where the owner is (RN-626)

    RN-629 — The QA Lead treats the subagent that SUSPENDS again on resumption the same way as the first time, and a result it does not know becomes a named block

    qa-automacao runs several commands and each one may ask for human approval. The QaLeadServer already stopped the area when run/5 returned {:awaiting, _} (finding AB, ADR 0052), but the RESUMPTION (retomar/3, when the decision arrives) returned the result of a loop that could suspend ANOTHER time — and that second {:awaiting, _} went into the list of collected results as if it were a verdict. On consolidating, registrar_resultado/5 had no clause for it and the GenServer crashed (FunctionClauseError, measured on 29/09/2026: 06:55:01 and 07:00:10), leaving the three delivered tasks in awaiting_qa forever, with no verdict and no named outcome.

    1. A single place decides what to do with a subagent's result (tratar_resultado/6), whether it comes from run/5 or from retomar/3. {:awaiting, _} keeps the in-flight state (keyed by the new action), keeps the gate_states row in in_progress with the subagent and STOPS the area: no verdict, without blocking the task, without recording the delegation. The resumer is the same {:action_settled, _} of Engine.Dev.Wake (ADR 0086), as many times as needed.
    2. A result the lead does not know never crashes nor vanishes (RN-059): it becomes {:blocked, …} with origin codigo (a clause is missing here, it is not infra, model or policy), which registrar_resultado/5 records as a failed delegation and QaLead.consolidar/1 turns into a block of the task with the origin.

    Declared, not resolved: an engine restart with the lead suspended loses the Wake subscription (accepted gap of ADR 0086); the GateRescuer restarts the whole area (ADR 0067), it does not resume the ctx. This rule does not change ADR 0090 (the moment of QA-strategy) nor the subagent's iteration ceiling.

    RN-636 — The AppSec only offers the threat model to whoever has not received an offer yet nor is active

    The AppSec created three offers per story with run_design, to the same three targets — N stories, 3N pending offers (AT-292, which merges the vault's AT-273 by owner decision; ADR 0182).

    1. It asks in seAusente mode. criar_handoffs_appsec/3 calls EngineApiClient.create_handoff_if_absent/5, which sends seAusente: true to the SAME internal route. The api decides under the target's lock (RN-635): with a pending offer in ANY session of the project, it returns the existing one (desfecho: ja_oferecido) and does not supersede it; with the target active, 409 agente_ja_ativo. Asking beforehand, through the engine, would race with another offer.
    2. "Already handled" is not a failure. The two responses above go on without agent.error. A real failure (5xx, network) is still narrated per target, with origin (RN-116, RN-361).
    3. The artifact stays. The artifact.threat_model is recorded BEFORE and regardless; what stops being born is the repeated offer.

    The dev agent's PR targets dev, and the gate judges that PR's diff (RN-664)

    RN-664 — The agents' work is born from dev, their PR targets dev, and the gate judges the diff against dev — the three together

    The product's branch policy has work born from dev (the bootstrap creates dev and qa from main, RN-582), but until now nobody pointed the agents at it: the dev agent's pr_open carried no targetBranch and the api filled in repo.defaultBranch (main); the Arquiteto's ADR PR was born from main and targeted main; the project's working tree opened default_branch; the gate computed the diff against it; and the QA context said "Repositório · branch main" (AT-250, item A4 of the real use of 09/29). Every agent PR skipped the dev → qa → main pipeline.

    Owner decision (09/30): the THREE now use dev, together — changing only one would leave the gate judging a diff that is not the PR's. The WORKING branch is dev and it is NOT the provider's default_branch (which remains the one promotion reaches last and is still read where it is the question):

    • the project's working tree opens dev (ensure_remoto/2 ignores the remote's default_branch to choose the base), and each dev agent's worktree is born from dev EXPLICITLY, not from HEAD — a workspace initialized before this rule is stuck on default_branch (the ready mark prevents re-initializing) and gets the local dev from origin/dev, without a new fetch;
    • the dev agent's pr_open carries targetBranch: "dev" in the payload, and the api, without the field (an action proposed before), fills in dev and no longer the defaultBranch; the ADR PR is born from dev and targets dev;
    • the gate's diff is dev...HEAD, and the project context line says "Repositório · branch de trabalho dev".

    Repository WITHOUT dev (adopted without bootstrap, or a bootstrap that did not reach create_dev_branch): there is NO fallback to default_branch — it would be the agent working and the gate judging over a base that is not the PR's. Each point fails NAMED, through the behavior it already had for a missing branch, with one new exception: the working tree refused nothing and created an EMPTY LOCAL dev (the path of the bare that never received a push), marked ready and never re-initialized again. Now that path only applies to a remote WITHOUT any branch; with branches and without origin/dev, initialization raises with the named message (mensagem_sem_branch_de_trabalho/1, "o repositório do projeto não tem a branch dev… crie a branch… e tente de novo") and .git is undone (AT-112), so the next attempt, with dev created, initializes normally. The worktree refuses with the same message when there is neither dev nor origin/dev; the diff fails with git's error naming the revision; the provider refuses the PR (and the creation of the ADR branch) naming the ref. infra (open_infra_pr) did NOT change: it still is born from and targets defaultBranch.

    The value lives in TWO places, on purpose, one per language, and they change together: BRANCH_DE_TRABALHO in the api and branch_de_trabalho/0 in the engine.

    The language of the agents' answers (RN-618..620, RN-622..624, ADR 0177)

    RN-667 — The structured form follows the reply language: the tool description pins no language at all

    The paid validation of AT-167 found, for an author with en preference (C06/C12), the ask_structured_questions call with prose in English and the form questions in Portuguese — in treated DeepSeek, 8 of the 15 replies of C06+C12. The cause is the product's, and lives in one place only: the tool description said label was "the question text, in pt-BR". It was a SECOND language guidance, fixed, outside the facade of RN-622, competing with it on every call of the Criativo and the PO (both use the SAME spec).

    1. The mechanism is removing the competing guidance, not adding another. The description now says "the question text, in the language of your reply (also applies to options)", and the example, which stays in Portuguese, is marked "the texts follow the language of your reply". It does not name ANY language: WHICH language it is remains decided only by the ephemeral message of Engine.Harness.IdiomaDaResposta, and the EngineApiClient facade does not change.
    2. The RN-622/623 guidance stays byte for byte. Zero extra tokens in the 50 of the ceiling. ask_structured_questions stays OUTSIDE @ferramentas_de_artefato (it is a question to the person of the turn, RN-623 item 4), so an en author in a pt-BR project gets the questions in English — which is what is wanted.
    3. Cost, where it falls. The description travels on every call that carries the tool, with any author: measured with gpt-tokenizer, only the text that changed, +23 tokens in cl100k and +19 in o200k (the label line 18/17 → 29/27; the example's 8/6 → 20/15). It is not the guidance moving into the description — the sentence points to it without naming a language, and what went away was precisely the sentence that named one.
    4. Guard. No harness tool description (the 22 of Engine.Harness.Tools.*) may cite pt-BR, (en), "in English", "em português" or "em inglês" — that of emit_artifact, which requires the payload KEYS "in ENGLISH", is left out because it speaks of a contract, not of text for the person.

    What this rule does NOT close, declared: the paid before/after measurement did not run (no OpenRouter key on the machine of the change) — the "before" is AT-167's (treated DeepSeek: 8 of 15 replies of C06+C12 with the form in Portuguese), and the "after" is an owner's pending item: pnpm --filter @brabo/scripts idioma:validar --arquivo-de-chave ~/.config/brabo/openrouter-test.env --bracos tratamento --casos C06,C12 --rodadas 5 --teto-usd 0.5 --saida ~/.cache/brabo/validacao-idioma/at-282, followed by idioma:validar --relatorio --saida in the same folder and the review of the form (formulario label of idioma:revisar). Suggested ceiling US$ 0.50: the whole treated arm cost US$ 0.21 (DeepSeek) and US$ 0.94 (Haiku) over 17 cases, and these are 2. The script reads the description from the product file, so it measures the new text without a copy. If the form stays in Portuguese with the neutral description, the next step is a conditional clause in the guidance (only when the call carries ask_structured_questions), and it has to fit in the worst case of RN-623 (44 / 37), which is already at ~48 with the frame.

    The Infra Lead does not announce a start it did not make (RN-668)

    RN-668 — What the Infra Lead's turn announces about starting the container is decided by the code, not by the model's text

    It is RN-163 ("nothing is announced that the code will not execute") applied to the Infra Lead. In the real use of 09/29 (session be70, seq 282) he wrote that he would start the container "in parallel" to the infra PR, and no start tool.call came afterwards (AT-264). There were two causes in the code, and both close here:

    1. The response's whole batch is dispatched before the turn ends. An accepted propose_infra_pr ends the turn without a new trip to the model — it is the only point where the CODE cuts the loop with the model wanting to continue —, but it stopped dispatching in the MIDDLE of the batch: a propose_container_start (or container_start_via_runner) requested in the SAME response, after the PR, vanished without tool.call, without tool.result and without notice. Now it is the Dev Lead's form (propose_execution_plan): all calls of the response run, and only afterwards does the accepted PR end the turn. A second propose_infra_pr in the same response is refused with a reason (tool.call + tool.result with ok: false) — only the first consolidates with Workflows.
    2. The turn's closing says, with a SERVER sentence, when the start was NOT proposed — the form of the Criativo's consolidated outcome (encerrar/2). The decision is by what the loop executed (was any start proposal ACCEPTED by the api in this turn?), never by what the model wrote; its text is not read. It speaks in two cases: the turn ended through the PR and no start was proposed; or a start was attempted, refused, and no accepted proposal came afterwards. A turn that never touched the start (a question, a gate correction) gets no sentence at all, and neither does a container already REGISTERED running/provisioning (the same local read as recusa_ja_de_pe/2, without HTTP).

    The kickoff and the description of propose_infra_pr now say what the code does: the start comes BEFORE the PR or in the SAME response, because the PR ends the turn. That is help for the model, not the guarantee — the guarantee is the closing of item 2.

    Nothing here starts a container or proposes a start, with the refusals by mode and state intact (RN-566/RN-610). Since RN-671 (AT-260) the first start is a SERVER step in the kickoff, marked by the SAME registrar_subida: in the common case it was proposed and the closing of item 2 has nothing to say; it still speaks when the server's start was refused (or DENIED by the policy, which since then counts as a refusal) and when it was not up to the server (no routing, runner) and the model did not propose it.

      (dispatch_calls), :539 (recusa_pr_repetida_no_lote), :1140 (registrar_subida), :1150 (fechar_subida), :1154 (desfecho_da_subida); apps/engine/lib/engine/infra/tools/propose_infra_pr.ex (the tool description)

    Credentialed git runs on the runner's host (RN-676)

    RN-673 — A message that arrives with a turn in progress enters a queue persisted in the log and is read at the end of the turn, N messages in a single turn

    Until now a message that reached a conversational agent in the middle of a turn was 409 turno_em_andamento (RN-578): recorded as chat.message (the api records before talking to the engine) and never read. The real use of 09/29 measured three in a single session — PO, Infra Lead and Dev Lead. Owner decision (10/01), for the SEVEN conversational agents through the same path:

    1. Queue, not refusal. With a turn in progress, the message enters the agent's queue (TurnoAssincrono.receber_mensagem/4), the agent records chat.message_queued (mensagemId, texto, idioma, posicao) and the click gets 202 with {entrega: "enfileirada", posicao}. Without a turn, the turn starts right away, as always (entrega: "lida"). The api route also returns the mensagemId — the id of the chat.message.
    2. N messages = 1 turn. At the END of the turn (finalizar/1, never suspender/1) the pending ones become ONE turn, in order of arrival, in a single text (FilaDeMensagens.texto_do_turno/1: one is itself; several, numbered), and the agent records chat.message_delivered with the ids. Delivery is a message to itself, after the server's closing (the Infra Lead's gate correction and the Arquiteto's handoff come first). A message that arrives between the end of the turn and the delivery goes to the END of the queue, and the queue is delivered right away.
    3. Ceiling of 10 pending per agent and session. The 11th is 409 fila_de_mensagens_cheia, with a durable agent.error of origin politica — it is recorded and will not be read.
    4. Cancel. POST …/agents/:agent/messages/:messageId/cancel (developer, the message route's minimum): only the SENDER (403 mensagem_de_outra_pessoa), only in a session that accepts conversation (RN-581). Whether it is still in the queue is decided by the agent's process, which serializes the race with the delivery: 409 mensagem_fora_da_fila when it was already read or cancelled. The engine records chat.message_cancelled on behalf of whoever cancelled; with the agent down, it records directly (the queue is the log).
    5. Persisted in the log, never in a table. Pending = queued and neither delivered nor cancelled — the same derivation in the engine and on the screen. The init/1 of the seven rebuilds the queue and schedules delivery; RN-580 removes from the history the cancelled one and the agent's own pending one; at boot, after closing the orphan turn (RN-586), the agent with a pending queue is woken up. The interrupted turn is still never redone.
    6. A turn suspended on approval (Dev Lead, RN-284) keeps refusing with aguardando_aprovacao — with no foreseeable end, queuing would promise a reading with no date. A queue that already existed waits for the resumption to finish.
    7. The screen. With a turn in progress the composer no longer locks for a message to an agent: the button becomes "Pôr na fila" and the message does not arm a new turn. In the thread, "na fila de " with Cancelar for the author, and "cancelada" struck through. The chat without an agent still locks.

    The turno_em_andamento refusal remains for what is not speech: the PO's story review, the Criativo's readiness, the Arquiteto's handoff offer.

    • Origin: AT-267 (item A21 of the analysis of the real use of 2026-09-29), owner decision of 10/01; ADR 0191

    The test plan is born after the dev's delivery (RN-674, ADR 0192)

    RN-674 — The QA-strategy writes the plan over the DELIVERED code, at the start of the qa-verificada cycle, and implementavel is judged without it

    In the real use of 09/29 the PRE-DEV test plan (RN-341) ended twice in toolloop.limit_reached 8/8 without emit_plano_de_teste: the loop read docs, empty RAG and folders until concluding that "the code does not exist yet". The owner decided (10/01) for option (b): the plan is born AFTER delivery. ADR 0192 revises 0090 on that point, and the rule has four parts:

    1. The plan is the first step of the review cycle. The QaLeadServer's run_area/3 obtains the delivery's plan BEFORE the subspecialties (plano_de_teste_da_entrega/5): it finds again in the session tail (rehydration ceiling, RN-580) the artifact.plano_de_teste of the SAME taskId — the correction round does not pay for another loop —, and otherwise runs QaEstrategiaAgent.run/6 with the dev's worktree as workspace_root, the task's business rules and the list of files the delivery touched (git diff dev...HEAD, up to 40 names). A diff that fails becomes text ("não consegui listar: motivo"), never an empty list.
    2. The plan is INPUT, never a verdict. It reaches the Automação at the END of its message, for the step of finding which test covers each rule; the verdict yardstick does not change (one coverageMatrix row per story rule, approved only with a green suite and every rule covered) and the external contract remains ONE qa_verdict per cycle. A plan that FAILS does not hold the review: the QA-strategy records agent.error with origin (RN-059) and the Automação receives the usual message, byte for byte.
    3. implementavel is judged over the story and the module_map. run_assessment/2 neither reads nor triggers a plan: it proposes the verdict on the FIRST call, over what the Dev Lead's kickoff already gives it, with payload storyId/parecer/justificativa. Gate, owner, severity warn and human approval do not change; only the entrada ([story-ready, module_map]). The appsec (RN-539) keeps triggering there, in design.
    4. The ceiling stays 8. "qa-estrategia" gets no clause in Iteracoes.tipo/1; what changed was the input. The agent now runs under the task_budget_micros that the subspecialties share (RN-036).

    run_design/3, Dispatcher.run_qa_estrategia/3 and QaEstrategiaContext were removed, and artifact.plano_de_teste now requires taskId. Declared and not decided here: whether the plan's executable criteria should become MANDATORY rows of the coverageMatrix (and therefore fail a delivery).

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/qa_lead_server.ex:190 (plano_de_teste_da_entrega), :715 (plano_de_teste_da_entrega), :716 (plano_ja_emitido), :721 (arquivos_alterados); apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/qa_estrategia_agent.ex:86 (run), :104 (token_budget_micros), :188 (descrever_arquivos); apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/qa_automacao_agent.ex:207 (com_o_plano); apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/dev_lead_tools.ex:457 (run_assessment), :538 (propor_parecer); apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/artifact_schemas.ex:59 (taskId); docs/gates.yml (qa-verificada, implementavel); docs/fluxo.yml (qa-estrategia, area-qa, dev-lead)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/gates/qa_lead_server_test.exs, describe "plano de teste da entrega (ADR 0192)" — the plan before the Automação and inside its message (happy path), reuse by taskId, another task's plan that does not count, and the plan that fails without holding the review (failure case); apps/engine/test/engine/gates/qa_estrategia_agent_test.exs (the delivery's files in the message, the diff that failed, taskId in the artifact); apps/engine/test/engine/agents/dev_lead_tools_test.exs, describe "assess_implementability (ADR 0090, insumo do ADR 0192)" (verdict on the first call, payload without a plan); dev_lead_server_test.exs ("assess_implementability pending TAMBÉM suspende"); apps/engine/test/engine/harness/artifact_schemas_test.exs ("sem taskId é rejeitado"). ExUnit: proven only in CI (repo.hex.pm gave 403 in the round's environment).
    • Origin: AT-269 (item A23 of the analysis of the real use of 09/29); owner decision on 10/01

    The Anamnese switched back on: eligible subject and profile fact (RN-680)

    RN-680 — The Anamnese does not run without an eligible subject, and the hypothesis the person themselves accepted becomes a fact of their profile in the graph and enters the context of the agents that talk with them

    Owner decision on 10/01 (AT-277): switch the Anamnese back on with fixes. In the real use of 09/29 it ran six times and all six ended in anamnese.run_skipped ("no eligible member"), written by the MODEL after a paid call (8,933 micros), and the three accepted hypotheses stayed in the queue with no destination. See ADR 0196.

    1. Without an eligible subject, the round does not run. A subject is an EFFECTIVE member of the project (projectRole ?? workspaceRole, the yardstick of RN-471), outside the opt-out, with OWN interaction in what the round shows the model — an event of theirs with actor_kind: "user" in the window, or a decision of theirs. The guard comes BEFORE triage and not even the queued hypothesis gets past it. Without a subject: no LLM call and no RAG search; the reason goes NAMED to the engine log (sem_sujeito_elegivel, with nenhum_membro or nenhuma_interacao_propria and the numbers) and, in a manually requested round, also to the event log (anamnese.run_skipped with causa: "sem_sujeito_elegivel"). Through the tick it is only a log — it would be a notice every 15 minutes per project. The prompt now lists only the subjects.
    2. A project member is the EFFECTIVE one. Creating a project does not write project_members, and the Anamnese read only that table — the workspace owner was never a member. The Anamnese's three use cases now read listEffectiveMembers; listMembers remains only project_members.
    3. A hypothesis ACCEPTED by the person THEMSELVES becomes a profile fact. The psychologist.hypothesis_accepted carries projectId, sujeito (the author of the analyzed session), hipotese, sugestao and fatoDoPerfil, and the GraphEventTranslator — the same one as the projector and grafo:reprojetar — translates it into (:FatoDoPerfil {hypothesisId}), linked to the person (SOBRE) and to the project (NO_PROJETO). fatoDoPerfil is only true when the one who accepted is the subject: a fact about a person is never born from someone else's click, and the payload says why (motivoSemFato). A declined one is only recorded, as before. Acceptances recorded before this RN lack the fields and do not become facts.
    4. The fact enters the context of the agents that talk with the person. The author's message to a conversational agent carries THEIR facts in this project — read by QueryUserContextUseCase, scoped to the project, the 5 most recent, with the total beside them and the cut stated —, and the engine puts them in the turn as an EPHEMERAL system message, through the same path as the language (RN-622): never in the history, before the language guidance, with a ceiling of 2,000 characters. Graph down becomes a log, and the turn goes on without the facts. A turn without an author receives no facts.
    5. Switched back on. ANAMNESE_ENABLED returns to default true in runtime.exs and in the three composes; START_ANAMNESE does not change (the production one stays false, an already declared divergence). The Psicólogo stays paused.
    • Code: apps/engine/lib/engine/anamnese/elegibilidade.ex:59 (avaliar); apps/engine/lib/engine/workers/anamnese_worker.ex:63 (maybe_analyze), :97 (narrar_sem_sujeito); apps/api/src/domain/iam/membros-efetivos.ts:16 (membrosEfetivos); apps/api/src/infrastructure/persistence/drizzle/project.repository.ts:209 (listEffectiveMembers); apps/api/src/application/use-cases/execution/accept-hypothesis.use-case.ts:75 (fatoDoPerfil); apps/api/src/application/graph-projection/graph-event-translator.ts:171 (projectProfileFact); apps/api/src/application/use-cases/graph/query-user-context.use-case.ts:68 (fatos); apps/api/src/domain/graph/perfil-do-autor.ts:30 (textoDoPerfilDoAutor); apps/api/src/application/use-cases/agents/send-agent-message.use-case.ts:111 (resolverPerfil); apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/perfil_do_autor.ex:68 (anexar); apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/idioma_da_resposta.ex:253 (com_idioma_do_autor); apps/engine/config/runtime.exs:315 (anamnese_enabled?)

    The RN-418 ceiling also applies through the typed door (RN-689)

    RN-690 — The assistant keeps the response's toolCalls: the tool result does not reach the provider orphaned

    The conversational agents with their own loop (Criativo, PO, Arquiteto, Dev Lead, UX Designer, Staff and Infra Lead) kept in the history the assistant's message WITHOUT the response's toolCalls and, right after, the role: "tool" message with the toolCallId. On the wire, the provider received a result that answered no call and discarded it: the model never saw what the tool returned and repeated the same call up to the loop ceiling (measured in v7.0.0: the PO called listar_regras_de_negocio 12 of 12 times in two turns).

    The assistant's message now carries the response's NATIVE toolCalls when there are any — the same format the ToolLoop already kept and that the ContextManager groups on compaction —, assembled in a single place. A response without toolCalls does not get the key. A call recovered from text (ToolCallRecovery, null id) stays out: there is no id to match. The api already passed the field on to the providers (toWireMessage in OpenAI-compatible, tool_use in Anthropic); the rehydration still brings tools as text.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/mensagem_do_assistente.ex:17 (de); apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/po_server.ex:280, and the same point in the other six servers
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/po_server_test.exs:93 (the second call carries the assistant with toolCalls right before the tool that answers it; the final answer, without a tool, does not get the key — failure case)
    • Origin: AT-350

    RN-695 — The Anamnese's analysis does not enter the session thread

    Measured in the real use of v7.0.0: the text the Anamnese's model writes during the round ("Analisando a janela do log… usuário (09824667-…)… Decisão de proficiência") appeared as a BUBBLE in the ideation session's thread, between the Criativo and the PO, with the user's raw UUID. The Anamnese does not converse with anyone: it analyzes the PERSON, and what it produces is the profile (RN-680).

    The rule: the round's text is recorded as anamnese.analysis, never as agent.response. The ToolLoop accepts response_event in the context (default agent.response, unchanged for every other consumer) and the Anamnese sets it. The session thread draws NO event whose actor is the anamnese agent — including the agent.response recorded before the fix. The session's log panel keeps showing its events; the result lives in Configurações › Perfil de proficiência, without a raw id. No "profile updated" marker enters the thread: it is left out until someone asks.

The catalog comes back intact on the following turn; the normal turn keeps emit_artifact and ask_structured_questions.

    RN-698 — The turn's text becomes ONE answer, and the iteration ceiling appears in the thread

    With tools, the model writes in pieces — a stretch before the call, another on the next round, often continuing the same sentence. Each round became its own agent.response, and the thread collapses the earlier ones into "Passos do turno": on show stayed only the last piece (" histórias e tarefas que cubram tudo.", starting with a space). The transport lost nothing — the api's final frame carries the whole text of the ROUND —; what was split was the TURN. In the seven conversational agents, the text of a round with a tool is accumulated in the turn's process and recorded in a single agent.response only when the turn closes (no tool, ceiling, failure, Dev Lead suspension, Infra PR). A piece that starts or ends with a space goes in as it came; two pieces stuck together become paragraphs.

    The conversational agents' toolloop.limit_reached (RN-166) now becomes a line in the thread, in the failure bubble, origin modelo, stating the ceiling ("parou no teto de N passos sem terminar o turno"); without the number in the payload the line says ?.

    The turn's agent.response carries modelName (RN-146) in EVERY outcome, not only in the tool-less closing: when the turn closes without a final call that brings it (it delivered an ask_structured_questions form, exhausted the ceiling, failed), the model of the last accumulated round applies. With no LLM call having returned a model, the field stays nil — never an invented model (AT-384; the Infra Lead records modelName in no outcome and stays out).

    Revision (owner decision, 10/03, AT-395): only the turn's closing in the bubble. In real use (loja-teste-2, 10/02) the single answer joined the text of ALL the rounds ("Vou registrar… Excelente! Registrei…", two summaries in a row in the PO). The turn's agent.response is still ONE, but content now is only the CLOSING — the text of the last round that wrote something (a turn that ends in a form, with no text in the last round, keeps the previous one) — and the text of the earlier rounds goes, in order, in the passos field, absent when there are none. Nothing vanishes: the thread draws each step as narration of the same turn+author before the closing, and the "Passos do turno" collapse that already existed folds them, closed by default; the steps do not count in the thread's message cut (RN-644). The rehydration (RN-580) returns passos BEFORE content, in the same assistant, so the model does not lose what it said mid-turn. It applies to the seven conversational agents (the Infra Lead too, without modelName). No ceiling of its own for passos: the accumulation was already recorded whole before the revision.
